    [
      "General salary information: The employee receives a salary based on one of the salary scales belonging to function groups 5 to 80. The mentioned amounts are based on a full-time working week of 1878 hours per year (average 36 hours per week). For employees with a working week deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies."
    ],
    [
      "Salary payment: The employee must have access to their salary for that month no later than two days, excluding Sundays and public holidays, before the end of the calendar month. Compensation for irregular service, on-call, standby, and call-out service, consigned break during night shifts, commuting, and travel and accommodation expenses in a month will be paid no later than the following calendar month."
    ],
    [
      "Function Valuation Healthcare (FWG): The computer-aided FWG® system is part of this CAO and is periodically updated. CAO parties determine the active system version. Employees can access the 'for inspection' version upon request. Classification or reclassification is based on an up-to-date job description meeting quality requirements. Rules and procedures in Appendix D, paragraph 2, apply. Employers and employees bound by the generally binding declaration have the right to inspect the FWG® system and its relation to function group classification."
    ],
    [
      "Classification in the salary scale: The employer classifies the employee's function using the FWG® system into one of the function groups 5 to 80. The salary scale number corresponds to the function group. Placement in the salary scale is based on experience gained elsewhere or not."
    ],
    [
      "Application of salary scales (expires as of January 1, 2014): For functional salary scales 5 to 20, one function year is required; for 21 to 40, two function years; for 41 to 60, three function years. Employees without the required function years are placed in the entry salary scale. As of January 1, 2013, all entry scales and the application of paragraphs 1 and 3 are abolished. Employees younger than 22 are placed in the youth scale. As of January 1, 2012, scales for 21-year-olds are abolished; as of January 1, 2013, for 20-year-olds; as of January 1, 2014, for 17, 18, and 19-year-olds. As of January 1, 2014, all youth scales and the application of paragraph 2 are abolished. The employer can deviate from the required function years, extending the entry scale application if function performance warrants it, with written and motivated notification to the employee."
    ],
    [
      "Reclassification of already classified functions: Reclassification occurs due to changes/additions to FWG 3.0® or substantial changes in function content. If reclassification results in a lower function group, the employee can reach the maximum salary of the lower group plus 10%. If the salary exceeds this, it is frozen. The final or frozen salary is adjusted with general wage adjustments. Reclassification to a higher function group applies the higher scale, retroactive to the agreement on the job description. The new salary must be at least the old salary, or the next higher amount in the new scale. Reclassification to a higher function group is not considered a promotion. Job re-description follows Protocol FunctieWaardering Gezondheidszorg® (Appendix D)."
    ],
    [
      "Periodic increases: Unless otherwise specified in the employment contract, a salary increase within the entry or functional scale is granted once a year. The periodic date is generally the date of commencement of employment. For commencement in the middle of a month, it's the first of the following month. For employees in the youth scale, it's the first of the month of their birthday. This changes upon promotion or after obtaining a diploma for a first healthcare training. If a performance appraisal system (based on Statuut Sociaal Beleid) gives cause, the employer may decide to grant no or multiple salary increases in a year."
    ],
    [
      "Gratuity and allowances: The employer can grant a gratuity or temporary allowance based on, for example, excellent performance or the labor market situation. These do not have a structural character and are not counted towards the pensionable salary."
    ],
    [
      "Promotion: Upon promotion to a function in a higher function group, the new salary is based on that higher scale. The old salary is increased by two periodic increments, then placed in the higher scale. If two increments are not possible due to reaching the scale maximum, the scale is extended with necessary inclusion table numbers. The new salary cannot exceed the maximum of the higher function's scale. The employer can agree on a different arrangement with the works council."
    ],
    [
      "Acting: An employee acting fully or almost fully in a higher-graded function, at the employer's request, receives remuneration. If acting for at least half of the daily working hours, the pro-rata principle applies to remuneration. The acting allowance is the difference between the current salary and the starting salary of the higher function, with a minimum of two periodic increments. If two increments are not possible due to reaching the scale maximum, the scale is extended with necessary inclusion table numbers. The total salary (including allowance) cannot exceed the maximum of the acting function's scale. Acting for vacation or less than one month is not compensated. The employer can agree on a different arrangement with the works council. The acting allowance is not structural and not pensionable."
    ],
    [
      "Service year gratuity: An employee continuously employed by the employer is entitled to a one-off gratuity of: a quarter of the monthly salary for 12.5 years of service; half of the monthly salary for 25 years of service; a full monthly salary for 40 years of service. If the contractual working hours changed during the relevant service years, the salary is proportionally increased or decreased."
    ],
    [
      "Gratuity at end of employment: When the employment contract ends due to reaching the old-age pensionable age, the employee is entitled to a gratuity equal to half of the salary. This also applies when the employment ends due to using the bridging benefit (OBU) or Flex-pension from the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn. Payment occurs if no new employment is entered into. If the employee enters into new employment with the same employer alongside the OBU, the gratuity is granted at the end of the new employment contract, based on the working hours and ip-number prior to using the OBU. If the employee uses FLEX-pension and remains employed, the gratuity is granted at the end of the employment contract, based on working hours and ip-number prior to using FLEX-pension. If the employee uses the exchange-OBU, the gratuity is granted when the employee takes full OBU and no new employment contract is entered into, based on working hours and ip-number prior to using the exchange-OBU."
    ],
    [
      "Employees in training: Articles 7.2.2 and 7.2.3 apply to inservice and BBL training, which consist of a vocational preparatory period (bvp) and subsequent practical training years."
    ],
    [
      "Vocational preparatory period (bvp): During the bvp, a training agreement is entered into. This period is not part of the first practical training year. The student is entitled to a monthly remuneration as stated in Appendix 4. This remuneration is 97% of the applicable gross minimum (youth) wage. Tuition fees are paid by the employer. Learning materials are provided on loan. The bvp for inservice training cannot exceed six months."
    ],
    [
      "Practical training years: After completing the bvp, the student is admitted to the first practical training year, with a training/employment contract. A maximum of seven lesson hours per day can occur in a lesson period. During practical training years, the student (except sterilization assistants and BBL students level 1 and 2) receives the salary stated in Appendix 2, or the minimum (youth) wage if higher. Sterilization assistants and BBL students level 1 and 2 receive the salary in Appendix 1, if the minimum (youth) wage is not higher. The student receives an annual periodic increase from the end of the bvp. Tuition fees are paid by the employer."
    ],
    [
      "HBO dual training: An HBO dual training consists of a first year (basic year) and practical training years. If a student interns during the basic year, they receive an internship allowance if Article 7.2.9 requirements are met. After the first year, a training/employment contract is entered into. During practical training years, the student receives the salary in Appendix 2, or the minimum (youth) wage if higher. If the second year is mainly theoretical, the student may receive the first practical training year salary for less than twelve months. The student receives an annual periodic increase from the date of commencement of employment."
    ],
    [
      "HBO-V dual training: Students in HBO-V dual training receive salary based on Article 7.2.4 paragraph 4 during the first two practical training years. During the third practical training year, the student receives the amount for the third practical training year for nine months, and for the remaining months, the amount for the fourth practical training year."
    ],
    [
      "Second healthcare training and follow-up training (expires as of January 1, 2013): Deviating from Articles 7.2.3 and 7.2.4, students already holding a healthcare diploma and employees pursuing a follow-up training are remunerated as follows: During the new training/employment contract, the student is scaled in the first year in the entry scale (seniority 0) of the lowest functional scale applied for the function for which the training is followed. The student-employee receives an annual periodic salary increase during the training. If the student-employee earned more before the training than the salary from paragraph 2, they receive a salary guarantee for the last earned salary until periodic increases lead to a higher salary. The employer can deviate from paragraphs 2 and 4 in consultation with the works council."
    ],
    [

The starting point is that the student's income does not decrease. In the absence of an institutional policy, the student retains the ip-number and progresses through the scale in which they were classified before the start of the training until the diploma of the training is obtained."

For salary determination, the hours actually worked are taken into account. Employer and employee can agree that lesson hours are fully or partially considered working time."
    ],
    [
      "Internship: Students for whom an internship is a mandatory part of their study are entitled to an internship allowance, provided the internship amounts to at least 144 hours per academic year. The internship allowance is not granted if a BBL student has an employment that entitles them to wage payment during the internship period. Students in the sense of this article are MBO and HBO students. The internship allowance for a full-time internship is € 308 per month (January 1, 2011). For a part-time intern, the pro-rata principle applies. The internship allowance is indexed annually with the consumer price index. The internship allowance includes the intern's incurred costs, with the exception of the allowance referred to in Article 12.1.1 paragraph 5, as it reads as of January 1, 2012. Costs for necessary vaccinations are reimbursed by the government or the employer."
    ],
    [
      "Training for medical specialist, hospital pharmacist, clinical chemist, clinical physicist: Employees undergoing training as a specialist (aios), hospital pharmacist, clinical chemist, or clinical physicist receive the salary stated in Appendix 3. Basic doctors in training for emergency medicine and sports medicine are remunerated accordingly. When scaling, relevant experience is taken into account; for each relevant year of experience, the first scale amount is increased by one periodic increment. Twelve months of relevant experience are required for an experience year. Experience that fully counts includes: work as a doctor-assistant in a comparable hospital function; work as a basic doctor in patient care in a comparable hospital function; work as a doctor-researcher in a scientific research project in a hospital; work as a basic doctor in education or research in a hospital. If the required months for an experience year are not completed on the date of commencement of employment, the periodic date is set so that the next periodic increment is granted when the experience year is completed, assuming good performance. The employee receives an annual periodic increase. If the agreed salary of a doctor prior to medical specialist training is higher than the salary from paragraphs 1 to 5, they retain that agreed salary until periodic increases lead to a higher salary."

      "Holiday allowance: The holiday allowance for employees who have been employed for a full year on May 31 is 8% of the annual salary. For this purpose, annual salary means: twelve times the salary applicable on May 1. For employees aged 22 or older, the holiday allowance for a full year of employment is at least € 1,699 (as of July 1, 2011), € 1,703 (as of October 1, 2011), € 1,729 (as of July 1, 2012), or € 1,763 (as of July 1, 2013). This minimum amount is increased in May of the payment year by any general wage adjustments and the effect of any increase in the holiday allowance percentage. If the employee has worked for only part of the period for which holiday allowance is calculated, or part-time, they are entitled to a pro-rata holiday allowance. If the employee leaves employment before the payment date, the holiday allowance is paid pro-rata based on the monthly salary at the end of employment for the number of months the employment lasted after May. Payment of the holiday allowance takes place in May, except for the aforementioned situation. The employer can, in consultation with the works council, make a different arrangement for employees who wish to receive their holiday allowance monthly, in which case it is 8% of the monthly salary."

If the Pension Accrual during Unemployment Scheme (FVP) applies, the provisions regarding the due premium in paragraph 1 do not apply to the unemployment benefit recipient. Paragraph 1 does not apply if the unemployment benefit recipient, who is younger than fifty years, can participate in the pension scheme associated with their new employment. If the employer's supplement is insufficient to cover the premium due by the unemployment benefit recipient, the latter must pay the missing part from the statutory benefit. The premium due on income above the contribution limit mentioned in the Pension Accrual during Unemployment Scheme is borne by the employer, after deduction of the proportional employee's share."

If holiday is taken on a day where a daily working time of nine hours would have been scheduled, then nine holiday hours are counted. The calculation result for holiday hours in any year is rounded up to whole hours. For employees with a working week deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies. The employee is entitled to 166 holiday hours per calendar year with retention of salary (144 statutory, 22 above-statutory). Employees using the 55-plus scheme or lgpb scheme (CAO 2008-2009) are entitled to 184 holiday hours. Holiday accrual is 1/12 per calendar month. Accrual continues for the last six months of incapacity for work. This also applies to partially incapacitated employees. Accrual during maternity/childbirth leave is granted independently. The employer can designate two working days as holiday leave, in consultation with the works council, and notify by the end of January. Holiday must be taken in the year of accrual, or the next year if not taken due to institutional interest. For partial incapacity, holiday can only be taken for the working portion. Holiday is granted according to employee wishes, with a minimum of three consecutive weeks. The employer can designate up to three sick days as holiday if the employee takes holiday during incapacity with occupational health physician's consent, ensuring at least 144 statutory holiday hours remain. General holiday arrangements require works council consent. Employees can cash out accrued holiday exceeding 144 hours. The employer can change holiday periods due to unforeseen circumstances affecting institutional functioning, in consultation with the employee, and must compensate for any resulting damage. If an employee reports sick during holiday, sick days from that point are not considered holiday days."

    [
      "Personal Life-Phase Budget (PLB): As of January 1, 2011, the employee receives a PLB of 30 hours for that calendar year. As of January 1, 2012, the employee annually receives a PLB of 57 hours. For commencement or termination within a calendar year, PLB is granted pro-rata. For employees with a working week deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies. The employer administers the PLB and provides an overview upon changes in employment percentage or termination. PLB is primarily for time-based spending to promote sustainable employability. Employees must request substantial leave (four months or more) at least three months in advance. The employer grants leave unless compelling business/service interests outweigh the employee's interest. Employees can take PLB leave before pensionable age for a maximum of half of the previous year's working hours, unless compelling business interests prevented earlier use. Part-time employees working extra hours generally receive PLB for these hours as a wage surcharge. PLB has no expiry date. Annually, starting December 2012, up to 22 PLB hours can be used in the Multi-Choice System of Employment Conditions (MKSA), without pro-rata application. Upon termination, death, or use in MKSA, accrued rights may be paid out. The total PLB value can be (partially) deposited into the life-course savings scheme upon termination. The value of an accrued hour is the employee's hourly wage at the time of payout. Upon termination, PLB is transferred to a new hospital employer if requested and if at least 50 PLB hours are available. If less than 50 hours, the employer informs the employee of the consequences. PLB accrual continues for a maximum of six months during illness/incapacity for work; periods are aggregated if interrupted by less than four weeks. Accrued leave does not expire during illness/incapacity for work."

    [
      "End of employment contract (as of January 1, 2012): Article 3.1.6 third bullet is amended as follows: 'On the day preceding the day on which the employee reaches the AOW/old-age pensionable age, unless another end date has been agreed upon between employer and employee prior to this date.'"
    ],
    [
      "End of employment contract: The employment contract ends: upon reaching the old-age pensionable age, unless another end date has been agreed upon; by termination, with due observance of Article 3.1.7; by dissolution by the court. The employment contract ends automatically: upon expiry of the term for which the employment contract was entered into, unless converted to an indefinite period contract under Article 7:668a BW; upon completion of the work for which the employment contract was entered into, unless converted to an indefinite period contract under Article 7:668a BW; when the employee dies."
    ],
    [
      "Termination: The employment contract is terminated in writing. A fixed-term employment contract can only be terminated prematurely if that right has been agreed upon in writing for each party. An employment contract may not be terminated, with the exception of termination for urgent reasons (Articles 7:678 BW and 7:679 BW), without the employer being in possession of a termination permit. The party terminating the employment contract must, upon request, provide written reasons for termination, even during an agreed probationary period. The effective date of dismissal is the first day of the calendar month. The employer cannot terminate during the period of illness, unless the incapacity has lasted for two years, or arose after a request for a dismissal permit was received by the CWI. The two-year period does not apply in situations described in Article 7:670b lid 3 BW. The employer conducts an exit interview with employees who wish to terminate their employment by resignation. If the statutory employee notice period is extended up to three months, the employer's period may be equal. For extensions to four, five, or six months, the employer's period is doubled. If the employer terminates, the statutory notice period applies if it is longer than the contractually agreed period. For employees employed on January 1, 1999, aged 45 or older, and for whom a longer notice period applied at that time, the old period remains applicable."
    ],
    [
      "Termination after childbirth and re-entry: If the employee terminates the employment contract in connection with childbirth no later than ten calendar days after childbirth, applicable notice periods do not need to be observed. The employment ends on the first day of the following calendar month. Employees who have terminated their employment in connection with childbirth or child care have the right to preferential treatment in a job application procedure at the institution where the employment ended, for a maximum of two years after dismissal."
    ],
    [
      "Death benefit: In case of death of the employee, the employer provides a death benefit to: a. the spouse or partner with whom the employee did not live permanently separated, and in the absence of these to b. his minor children, and in the absence of them to c. the person with whom the employee lived in a family context and whose living expenses he largely provided for. If the deceased leaves no relatives as mentioned in paragraph 1, the employer may grant the benefit or a part thereof to the person(s) deemed eligible based on fairness. The death benefit is paid based on the last earned salary of the employee for the period from the day after death up to and including the last day of the third month after the month in which the death occurred. The death benefit is reduced by any death benefit received under sickness or incapacity for work insurance and the Supplementary Benefits Act."
    ],
    [
      "Unemployment benefit (Wachtgeld): This chapter applies to employees whose employment contract does not end at their own request, starting from the day following the day on which the employment ends, if this termination occurs due to: complete or partial abolition of their function or formation place; reorganization whereby work activities have become completely or partially redundant; merger, liquidation, complete or partial closure of the institution; unsuitability of the employee, which is not due to their fault or doing. The employee must have reached the age of fifty and have been employed by the employer or their legal predecessor for fifteen years or longer. Unemployment benefit is granted if the employee has been granted a WW benefit and otherwise does everything necessary to obtain the benefits mentioned in Article 14.1. If the unemployment benefit recipient receives a ZW or WIA benefit on the day the unemployment benefit would start, this is supplemented to the amount and for the duration of the unemployment benefit. If no WW benefit is granted within two years, the unemployment benefit ends after two years at the latest. If an employee is notified in writing that their employment contract will be terminated for one of the reasons in paragraph 1, and they accept another employment before the dismissal date with a lower salary, they receive a supplement to that salary, equal to the unemployment benefit, from the start of the new employment."
    ],
    [
      "Duration of unemployment benefit: The unemployment benefit is granted for three months, increased by three months for each full year of service, with a maximum of twenty years of service taken into account. For employees who will reach pensionable age within five years after the dismissal date and have completed at least ten years of service, the unemployment benefit duration is extended until that age. For employees who qualify for the full bridging benefit (OBU) from Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn within five years after dismissal, the unemployment benefit duration is extended until that time, provided they have at least ten years of service and have voluntarily continued pension contributions. During extended unemployment benefit periods (paragraphs 2 and 3), Article 14.4 paragraph 1 applies. For calculating service years, all full, consecutive months of employment with NVZ, NZf, or their legal predecessors count. Service years with interruptions during which unemployment benefit was received are considered continuous. If unemployment benefit ended because the employee earned income from employment and this employment does not end at their own request for a reason mentioned in Article 14.2 paragraph 1, the unemployment benefit entitlement revives. The duration of the new employment is deducted from the total unemployment benefit entitlement. Revival of unemployment benefit does not apply if and as long as there is an entitlement to unemployment benefit with the new employer."
    ],
    [
      "Amount of unemployment benefit: The unemployment benefit is equal to the last earned salary for the first six months, then 80% for the next three months, 75% for the following two years, and then 70% of the salary. During the unemployment benefit period, it is never less than 80% of the minimum wage. If the recipient receives a bridging benefit from Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn, it is supplemented to the unemployment benefit amount. General wage adjustments applicable to this CAO, which the recipient would have received if employed, are considered in the unemployment benefit calculation. If gradual reduction of individual work leads to unfavorable results for the recipient, deviations from the above provisions are made in their favor."
    ],
    [
      "Reduction of unemployment benefit: If the unemployment benefit recipient earns income from work or business on or after the start of the unemployment benefit, these incomes are deducted from the unemployment benefit if, together with the unemployment benefit, they exceed the last earned salary. If a penalty reduction is applied to an employee's WW benefit, the employer's obligation to supplement (Article 14.4) will be adjusted accordingly, and the employee cannot claim wage payment under Article 7:629 BW. If income from business or work leads to a (partial) reduction of the statutory benefit, the portion of income deducted from the statutory benefit becomes part of the unemployment benefit."
    ],
    [
      "Forfeiture of unemployment benefit: Unemployment benefit is forfeited: upon the death of the employee; upon entitlement to old-age or invalidity pension from Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn, from the employment from which they were dismissed with unemployment benefit; if the unemployment benefit recipient fails to fulfill their obligations under Article 14.6; if a benefit mentioned in Article 14.1 is stopped because the unemployment benefit recipient fails to do what is necessary to obtain one of these benefits. During an appeal procedure under these statutory regulations, unemployment benefit payment is suspended. Unemployment benefit can be declared forfeited by the employer if: the unemployment benefit recipient fails to cooperate with a medical examination for invalidity pension or statutory incapacity for work benefit; the unemployment benefit recipient has settled permanently abroad. This provision does not apply to those who have reached the age of 55."
    ],
    [
      "Death benefit for unemployment benefit recipient: In case of death of the unemployment benefit recipient, the employer provides a benefit to the persons mentioned in Article 3.1.9 paragraph 1 or 2, equal to the unemployment benefit that would have been paid for the first three months following the month of death. This death benefit is forfeited if and insofar as death benefits are provided under social insurance laws."
    ],
    [
      "Payment of unemployment benefit: Unemployment benefit is paid monthly in accordance with the regulations for salary payment."
    ],
    [
      "Special unemployment benefit regulations: If the new employer has no relocation allowance scheme, employees maintaining their own household who relocate within one year after termination receive a one-off allowance equal to one monthly salary (Article 14.1 sub b), with a minimum of twice the applicable minimum wage per month, if the distance to the new workplace reasonably requires relocation. Existing commitments for study cost reimbursements remain valid during the unemployment benefit period. Study costs are 100% reimbursed if the training results from a placement within the institution due to reorganization or abolition of the function where the employee works. If the unemployment benefit recipient was an IZZ participant before dismissal, participation continues during the unemployment benefit period under Article 3.3.1."
    ],
    [
      "Unemployment benefit in case of institutional closure: In case of institutional closure, the employer is obliged to take timely measures to ensure unemployment benefit entitlements."

  "overtime_information": [
    [
      "Overtime: Overtime means work incidentally performed beyond the established working hours in the work and rest time schedule. The excess is measured on a semi-annual basis, starting from the moment of excess. Compensation for overtime is given if the employee has received an order to work overtime, or could reasonably assume they would have received an order to work overtime. In the latter case, the employer retrospectively determines the necessity of overtime."
    ],
    [
      "Scope and exempted employees for overtime: If overtime is performed for 30 minutes or less before or after scheduled working hours, this period is not compensated. If overtime is performed for more than 30 minutes, it is rounded up to a full hour. If overtime is performed for more than one hour, it is rounded up to half or full hours. Pregnant employees will not be assigned overtime after the third month of pregnancy, unless the employee agrees to perform overtime (Article 4:5 Atw)."
    ],
    [
      "Maximum number of overtime hours: The number of overtime hours may not exceed an average of 10% per week, measured quarterly: a. of 36 hours if the employee's salary does not exceed ip-nr 48; b. of 42 hours if the employee's salary exceeds ip-nr 48, but does not exceed ip-nr 74 of that table; c. of 52 hours if the employee's salary exceeds ip-nr 74. For employees with a working week deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies. If the 10% is exceeded, upon request of the employee, assistance is provided or a vacancy is created. If a part-time employee works more than 10% of their contractual working hours in any quarter, they are offered a contract for the additional hours upon request. If not requested, assistance is provided or a vacancy is created. The works council receives an overview of overtime hours per employee per calendar quarter within a department or group upon request, to assess the policy on vacancy creation or assistance provision."
    ],
    [
      "Compensation scheme for full-time employees: Compensation for overtime is provided, unless paragraph 3 states otherwise, in the form of time off, equal to the number of hours the overtime lasted, and furthermore in the form of a monetary payment as referred to in paragraph 2. The monetary payment consists of a percentage of the hourly wage: 25% for overtime between 06:00 and 18:00 on Monday to Friday (max five hours in seven days, remaining hours 50%); 50% for overtime between 18:00 and 22:00 on Monday to Friday; 75% for overtime on Saturday up to 24:00 and on free days; 100% for overtime on Saturday from 24:00, on Sundays and public holidays between 00:00 and 24:00 and on December 24 and 31 between 18:00 and 24:00. Free days are non-Sunday/holiday days when the employee is not scheduled to work. The right to overtime compensation (paragraph 1) is granted if: a. the employee's salary does not exceed ip-nr 48; b. the employee's salary exceeds ip-nr 48 but not ip-nr 74, if worked hours above scheduled hours exceed six per week on average; c. the employee's salary exceeds ip-nr 74, if worked hours above scheduled hours exceed sixteen per week on average."
    ],
    [
      "Compensation scheme for part-time employees: Compensation for overtime consists of the employee's applicable hourly wage, if and insofar as the number of overtime hours, averaged per week, measured over the period for which the work and rest time schedule applies, does not exceed the difference between the employee's applicable contractual working hours and the full-time working hours. Furthermore, the employee is entitled to accrual of holiday hours, holiday allowance, and, if applicable, an irregular service allowance on the relevant hourly wage. Compensation under Article 9.4 is granted if and insofar as the number of overtime hours, averaged per week, measured over a period for which the work and rest time schedule applies, exceeds the difference between the employee's applicable contractual working hours and the full-time working hours."
    ],
    [
      "Taking compensation for overtime: If the interests of the work, in the employer's opinion, prevent granting the time off referred to in Article 9.4, the time off is converted into a monetary amount, consisting of the proportional part of the salary. The works council receives insight into the application of the previous paragraph upon request."
    ],
    [
      "Doctor in training to become a specialist (aios) overtime: When determining whether overtime has occurred, all hours worked by the aios that fall under the definition of 'normative work week' as referred to in Article 6.13 must be included. Hours exceeding the normative work week of an average of 48 per week are compensated under Article 9.1. Deviating from Article 9.4 paragraph 2, employer and aios can agree to base the compensation on a weighted arithmetic average of the percentages of paragraph 2 over the total number of hours worked from the 48th hour."
    ],
    [
      "Irregular service: Irregular service means work performed according to the work and rest time schedule during the hours specified in Article 10.4, insofar as they do not exceed 36 hours. For employees with a working week deviating from the full-time norm, the contractually agreed number of hours applies instead of the 36-hour limit. If the employer deems it necessary to assign irregular service, compensation is granted according to this chapter."
    ],
    [
      "Scope and exempted employees for irregular service: Employees classified in function group 65 or lower are entitled to compensation for performing irregular service. Pregnant employees will not be assigned irregular service after the third month of pregnancy, unless the employee does not object. Employees aged 57 and older will not be assigned night shifts, unless the employee does not object. Employees who waive the right to exemption from night shifts as stated in paragraph 3 receive forty hours per year. These hours are deducted from the total annual working hours. For employees with a working week deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies."
    ],
    [
      "Compensation scheme for irregular service: Compensation for irregular service is provided in the form of a monetary payment or, if the employee requests it, in the form of time off. Time off is determined by dividing the monetary compensation calculated under Article 10.4 by the employee's hourly wage. Unless institutional interests prevent it, the request is granted by the employer."
    ],
    [
      "Calculation of compensation for irregular service: The monetary payment referred to in Article 10.3 is calculated based on the applicable hourly wage, with a maximum of the hourly wage derived from ip-nr 19, using the following percentages: 22% for irregular service during hours between 06:00 and 07:00 and between 20:00 and 22:00 on Monday to Friday; 38% for hours between 06:00 and 08:00 and between 12:00 and 22:00 on Saturday; 47% for hours between 00:00 and 06:00 and between 22:00 and 24:00 on Monday to Friday; 52% for hours between 00:00 and 06:00 and between 22:00 and 24:00 on Saturday; 60% for hours between 00:00 and 24:00 on Sundays and public holidays and for hours between 18:00 and 24:00 on December 24 and 31."
    ],
    [
      "Phasing-out scheme for irregular service allowance: If an employee's irregular service is terminated or reduced by the employer, or on medical advice not due to the employee's fault, they are entitled to compensation. This also includes promotion as per Article 7.1.9. Conditions for compensation: employee performed irregular service for at least three continuous years in the same institution; no temporary termination or reduction of irregular service; the salary difference (last earned salary plus average monthly irregular service compensation vs. new salary plus any remaining average monthly irregular service compensation) is more than 10% of the former. The compensation is calculated over the difference between: a. salary plus average monthly irregular service compensation from the past twelve months, and b. the new salary plus any remaining average monthly irregular service compensation (measured over three months after termination/reduction). This difference, reduced by 2% of the amount under sub a, forms the basis for compensation, which remains unchanged. Compensation is 75% in the first year, 50% in the second, and 25% in the third year."
    ],
    [
      "On-call, standby, and call-out service: On-call service means a continuous period of up to 24 hours where the employee, in addition to agreed work, must be reachable to perform agreed work as soon as possible upon call. Standby service means a continuous period of up to 24 hours where the employee, in addition to agreed work, must be present at the workplace to perform agreed work as soon as possible upon call. Call-out service means a period between two consecutive shifts or during a break, where the employee must only be reachable to perform agreed work as soon as possible upon call in unforeseen circumstances. For doctors in training to become specialists (aios), hours worked during these services count towards the normative work week (Article 6.13)."
    ],
    [
      "Scope and exempted employees for on-call/standby/call-out service: For nursing and care employees (Atb) and doctors, the applicable Atb norms apply. Article 4.8:2 paragraph 2 (customized arrangement/opt-out) of the Working Hours Decree is not used. During these services, only work that cannot be postponed may be performed. Employees are free from these services for at least two weekends per 28 consecutive days. If more than two hours of work are performed during on-call service between 00:00 and 06:00, or if an employee is called to the institution at least twice, they are entitled to at least six hours of uninterrupted rest immediately following the last period of actual work. Scheduled day shift hours that become rest time are paid by the employer. Pregnant employees will not be assigned these services after the third month of pregnancy, unless they do not object. Employees aged 58 and older will not be assigned these services between 00:00 and 06:00, unless they do not object."
    ],
    [
      "Working hours during on-call, standby, and call-out service: If an employee is called during standby service, compensation is calculated for at least 30 minutes. If called during on-call or call-out service, it's at least 30 minutes plus actual travel time. If work lasts longer than 30 minutes, it's rounded up to a full hour. If work lasts longer than one hour, it's rounded up to half or full hours. For aios, the number of hours worked during these services can be determined based on an average from experience data. The number of hours worked during these services may not exceed an average of 10% per week, measured quarterly: of 32 hours if the employee's salary does not exceed ip-nr 48; of 42 hours if the employee's salary exceeds ip-nr 48 but not ip-nr 74; of 52 hours if the employee's salary exceeds ip-nr 74. If the 10% is exceeded, upon request of the employee, assistance is provided or a vacancy is created. If a part-time employee works more than 10% of their contractual working hours in any quarter, they are offered a contract for the additional hours upon request. If not requested, assistance is provided or a vacancy is created. The works council receives an overview of hours worked under Article 11.1 per employee per calendar quarter within a department or group upon request, to assess the policy on vacancy creation or assistance provision."
    ],
    [
      "Compensation scheme for on-call, standby, and call-out service: Employees whose salary does not exceed the amount indicated under ip-nr 88 receive compensation in time off for hours spent in on-call, call-out, and/or standby service. This compensation per hour is: for on-call/call-out service: 1/24 on public holidays, 1/36 on Saturdays/Sundays, 1/48 on other days; for standby service between 06:00 and 24:00: 2/24 on public holidays, 1/24 on Saturdays/Sundays, 1/48 on other days; for standby service between 24:00 and 06:00: 3/24 on public holidays, 2/24 on Saturdays/Sundays, 1/24 on other days. If an employee performs on-call and/or call-out service on more than eight weekend days in a period of three consecutive 28-day periods, they receive a 50% surcharge on this compensation for the additional weekend services. Article 11.8 paragraph 3 does not apply to this surcharge."
    ],
    [
      "Compensation scheme for work during on-call, standby, and call-out service: Compensation for work performed during these services is provided, unless paragraph 4 states otherwise and with due observance of Article 11.4, in the form of time off, equal to the number of hours worked, and furthermore in the form of a monetary payment as referred to in paragraph 2. The monetary payment consists of a percentage of the hourly wage: 25% for work between 06:00 and 18:00 on Monday to Friday (max five hours in seven days, remaining hours 50%); 50% for work between 18:00 and 22:00 on Monday to Friday; 75% for work on Saturday up to 24:00 and on free days; 100% for work on Saturday from 24:00, on Sundays and public holidays between 00:00 and 24:00 and on December 24 and 31 between 18:00 and 24:00. Free days are non-Sunday/holiday days when the employee is not scheduled to work. Compensation (paragraph 1) is granted regardless of salary, as long as worked hours do not exceed an average of 36 per week. If this average is exceeded, compensation is granted if: a. salary does not exceed ip-nr 48; b. salary exceeds ip-nr 48 but not ip-nr 74, if worked hours above scheduled hours exceed six per week on average; c. salary exceeds ip-nr 74, if worked hours above scheduled hours exceed sixteen per week on average. Hours worked by an aios during these services that exceed the average 48-hour work week are compensated under paragraphs 1 and 2 of this article."

    ],
    [
      "Taking compensation for on-call, standby, and call-out service: Time off mentioned in Article 11.4 must be granted and taken within two months after the service, unless otherwise agreed. Time off mentioned in Article 11.5 must be granted and taken, after consultation with the employee, no later than the quarter following the quarter in which the work was performed, unless otherwise agreed. If institutional interests prevent granting time off, up to half of the time off from Article 11.4 can be converted into a monetary amount (proportional part of salary). Time off from Article 11.5 can be fully converted into money. The works council receives insight into this application upon request.     ],
    [
      "Maximum working hours: The working time per shift, excluding overtime, is a maximum of ten hours; including overtime, twelve hours. The working time per night shift, excluding overtime, is a maximum of nine hours; including overtime, ten hours. The working time per week is a maximum of sixty hours. The employer does not use the possibilities offered by the Atw (Article 5:8 paragraph 3) and Atb (Articles 4.7:1, 4.7:2 and 5.20:2) to extend working hours per shift, per night shift or per week. The working time per four weeks is a maximum of an average of 55 hours per week, and per sixteen weeks, a maximum of an average of 48 hours per week. The working time per sixteen weeks is a maximum of an average of forty hours per week if the employee performs sixteen or more night shifts in a sixteen-week period. For doctors in this situation, the working time is a maximum of an average of 48 hours per week in a thirteen-week period."
    ],
    [
      "Maximum number of night shifts: The employee may perform a maximum of five consecutive night shifts. At the employee's request, the employer can agree that the employee performs a maximum of seven consecutive night shifts, but no more than 43 night shifts per sixteen weeks. The employer does not use the possibility offered by the Atw (Article 5:8 paragraph 9) to increase the number of night shifts. For employees using the permanent night work transition provision (Article 8.1:1 Atb), paragraphs 1 and 2 of this article do not apply."
    ],
    [
      "Shifted shifts: The employer can incidentally and in special cases, if business interests necessitate it, make a change to the already established work and rest time schedule. A shifted shift occurs if a number of consecutive hours, during which the employee would have to work according to the work and rest time schedule, are shifted to another time when the employee would be free according to this schedule. If the employer makes a change to an already established work and rest time schedule and a shift occurs within 24 hours after the employee has been informed of this, the employee receives, in addition to the hourly wage for the hours of that shifted shift, only an overtime allowance percentage as referred to in Article 9.4 paragraph 2. In case of a change in the work and rest time schedule, the employee receives compensation for expenses already incurred in connection with leisure activities."

The normative work week of the aios can, in connection with training activities integrated into daily work, comprise more than an average of 7.6 hours per day and a maximum of 48 hours per week on average. Hours worked during on-call, standby, and call-out services count towards determining the normative work week."

  "training_information": [
    [
      "Training plan, career advice and facilitation: The employer annually establishes the training plan and the necessary budget for it, in consultation with the works council. The training plan includes function-oriented, profession-oriented and employability-oriented training and a facilities scheme. The employee has the right and obligation to participate in training activities. The employee's wishes regarding career and training activities and career aspirations are discussed in the annual appraisal (Article 3.2.2). The employee is entitled to individual career advice once every five years. When granting training activities (paragraph 3), the employer takes into account the outcomes of the career advice. The facilities scheme (paragraph 2) includes a study costs and study leave scheme, which at least addresses: studies requested by the employer are fully reimbursed; for part-time employees, the pro-rata principle for (partial) cost reimbursement is not applied; study leave is granted; repayment of already granted reimbursements. The facilities scheme states that the costs of training undertaken at the employer's request are fully reimbursed. The time spent on these trainings is considered working time."
    ],
    [
      "Study agreements: Parties agree to conduct research on a number of themes within the StAZ framework during the term of this CAO. The study agreements are not optional; if parties see reason to do so, the recommendations/conclusions from the research will be implemented. The themes for the agreed research are: 1. 'The New Way of Working': parties agree to investigate possibilities within institutions covered by the Hospital CAO to better promote 'the new way of working' (time and place independent) based on an inventory of good practices. 2. Training and Development: parties agree to investigate how training plans agreed upon with the works council within institutions are implemented, and how the allocated funds are used. 3. Wajong: parties agree to investigate how, within institutions covered by the Hospital CAO, in cooperation with the UWV Werkbedrijf, opportunities can be created for young people with a work disability (Wajongers) to qualify for vacancies that are realistic given their individual competencies and skills."

    [
      "Training for medical specialist, hospital pharmacist, clinical chemist, clinical physicist: Employees undergoing training as a specialist (aios), hospital pharmacist, clinical chemist, or clinical physicist receive the salary stated in Appendix 3. Basic doctors in training for emergency medicine and sports medicine are remunerated accordingly. When scaling, relevant experience is taken into account; for each relevant year of experience, the first scale amount is increased by one periodic increment. Twelve months of relevant experience are required for an experience year. Experience that fully counts includes: work as a doctor-assistant in a comparable hospital function; work as a basic doctor in patient care in a comparable hospital function; work as a doctor-researcher in a scientific research project in a hospital; work as a basic doctor in education or research in a hospital. If the required months for an experience year are not completed on the date of commencement of employment, the periodic date is set so that the next periodic increment is granted when the experience year is completed, assuming good performance. The employee receives an annual periodic increase. If the agreed salary of a doctor prior to medical specialist training is higher than the salary from paragraphs 1 to 5, they retain that agreed salary until periodic increases lead to a higher salary."

Learning materials are provided on loan. The bvp for inservice training cannot exceed six months."

During practical training years, the student (except sterilization assistants and BBL students level 1 and 2) receives the salary stated in Appendix 2, or the minimum (youth) wage if higher. The student receives an annual periodic increase from the end of the bvp. Tuition fees are paid by the employer."

If a student interns during the basic year, they receive an internship allowance if Article 7.2.9 requirements are met. After the first year, a training/employment contract is entered into. During practical training years, the student receives the salary in Appendix 2, or the minimum (youth) wage if higher. If the second year is mainly theoretical, the student may receive the first practical training year salary for less than twelve months. The student receives an annual periodic increase from the date of commencement of employment."

  "contract_type_information": [
    [
      "Duration of the employment contract: The employment contract is generally entered into for an indefinite period. This does not apply to the employment contract of employees who have already reached the old-age pensionable age upon commencement of employment. For a fixed-term employment contract, the reason or duration must be stated. Article 7:668a BW applies to a series of fixed-term employment contracts. The effect of Article 7:668a BW paragraph 1 sub b is excluded for temporary agency workers. If a temporary agency worker enters into a fixed-term employment contract with the same institution within three months after the end of the temporary agency contract, this is considered a second fixed-term employment contract. Article 7:668a BW paragraph 2 remains applicable. Based on Article 7:668a BW paragraph 5, the employer is permitted in the following exhaustively listed situations to extend a fixed-term contract without an employment contract for an indefinite period arising: If an employment contract for a definite period as a non-specialist doctor in training (anios) ends, a fixed-term employment contract as a specialist in training (aios) or as a doctor in training for a recognized medical follow-up training can be entered into for the duration of the training; If an employment contract for a definite period with regard to performing research ends, a new fixed-term employment contract, with regard to performing research, with a maximum duration of four years, can be entered into; If an employment contract for a definite period with regard to following a training ends, a new employment contract for the remaining duration of the training can be entered into, if the extension is necessary in connection with completing the training."

  "safety_information": [
    [
      "General obligations of employer and employee: The employer is obliged to do or refrain from doing all that a good employer should do or refrain from doing in similar circumstances. The employer is obliged to make known the policy regarding care-ethical issues, insofar as this is necessary for the employee's function performance. The employer provides the employee with the necessary personnel, instrumental and spatial facilities and ensures that the necessary professional literature is accessible, after consultation with the person concerned and department and within the possibilities of the institution. The employer will pursue an active policy regarding the participation of women in higher and managerial positions, including functions, agreements regarding training, career guidance, and training. The employer, within the framework of working conditions policy, pays attention to workload. If developments in the absenteeism percentage give cause for it, it will be investigated by or under the supervision of the occupational health service whether there is a relationship with the workload. If this relationship exists, appropriate measures will be discussed in consultation with the works council. For employees working in alternating shifts who indicate they can no longer perform these shifts, the employer, in consultation with the employee, seeks a suitable solution. The employer, in consultation with the works council, establishes a regulation within the framework of age-oriented personnel policy, which includes measures to lighten the workload of older employees. These measures may concern a transfer to another function or function adjustment."
    ],
    [
      "Employee complaints procedure: The employer establishes an employee complaints committee. In consultation with the works council, a general complaints procedure and a procedure for undesirable behavior will be established. The employer and the works council nominate an equal number of committee members, who are appointed by the management. The complaints committee issues a weighty written advice to the employer, of which the employee receives a copy."
    ],
    [
      "Insurance agreement: The employer is obliged to conclude an insurance agreement that covers the employee's personal civil liability for death, bodily injury and/or property damage, caused to third parties in the performance of their function, including damage caused to third parties by the person who supervises the employee in the work situation on behalf of the employer. The employer indemnifies the employee for liability in this regard and waives any possibility of recourse against the employee, except in cases where the damage is the result of intent or deliberate recklessness of the employee."
    ],
    [
      "Medical examination: The employee is obliged to undergo a medical examination deemed necessary by the employer with reasons, if it concerns an examination with a preventive effect on the health situation in the institution."

    [
      "Errors and incidents: With regard to the employee, the MIP-/FONA-reglement applies in the context of function performance."
    ],
    [
      "Whistleblower scheme: The employee can safely report any suspected abuses in the institution. For the procedure, reference is made to the Misconduct Reporting Scheme."
    ],
    [
      "Absenteeism policy: The employer pursues an active policy aimed at reducing absenteeism. The employer, in consultation with the works council, establishes an absenteeism reporting regulation, which at least specifies: when the employee must report sick to the employer in case of illness, also during holidays, explicitly; which instructions the employee must observe when reporting sick/incapacity for work; model control/guidance activities that the employer can carry out. To support an active policy aimed at reducing absenteeism, CAO parties have made recommendations in the 'Protocol Recommendations for improving working conditions in hospitals and combating absenteeism, incapacity for work and workload'."

    [
      "Protocol recommendations for improving working conditions in hospitals and combating absenteeism, incapacity for work and workload: CAO parties state that: good working conditions contribute to reducing health damage, absenteeism and incapacity for work, as well as to improving employee motivation and productivity; the regulation regarding working conditions obliges employers to generally ensure the safety, health and well-being of employees; the employee bears an individual responsibility for their own health; based on the Working Conditions Act, obligations are imposed on both the employer, the employee and the expert services; in the hospital sector, attention to working conditions needs further strengthening; absenteeism and the outflow to the WIA in the hospital sector can be reduced; reduction of absenteeism and strengthening of sustainable employability can contribute to improving the image for the hospital sector; systematic improvement of working conditions must be worked on continuously and consistently in the sector; good cooperation and structural consultation between employer and works council is important for reducing absenteeism, incapacity for work and workload; policy aimed at absenteeism, incapacity for work and workload is an integral part of the overall business policy. General: The Working Conditions Act imposes obligations on employers and employees. Furthermore, there are regulations for cooperation and consultation between employers, employees and expert services. CAO parties formulate principles and make recommendations in this appendix to promote cooperation between employer, works council and experts. Principles and recommendations: 1. Institutions apply the guidelines of the occupational health catalog. For topics not mentioned in the occupational health catalog, the Working Conditions Act applies. 2. The Risk Inventory and Evaluation (RIE) is an important aspect of occupational health policy. A systematically performed RIE is necessary, as is a corresponding action plan. 3. Special attention in the RIE is given to addressing workload, aggression, physical strain and violence. 4. It is important that an institution first establishes its own occupational health policy and formulates objectives based on it. Furthermore, within the institution, in line with the amended Working Conditions Act, a prevention officer will be appointed. Subsequently, it can be considered which expertise – internal and external – will be engaged. This provides opportunities to better align the occupational health policy with the institutional policy. 5. Within the framework of occupational health policy, it is desirable to have: an instrument for absenteeism monitoring; an occupational health service; periodic occupational health examination; a workplace survey; instruments for workload management; methods for workload management; a sickness and re-employment procedure; a re-integration policy; an occupational health annual plan; an occupational health coordinator; instruments for promoting mental and physical employability. 6. Part of the absenteeism policy is the quantitative determination of the extent of absenteeism at various levels of the company. Exceeding these norms must, in the opinion of the parties, lead to additional policy. 7. Employer and works council agree on which measures will be taken if the quantitative extent of absenteeism is higher than the goals set in consultation between employer and works council. 8. The employer ensures adequate support for employees who have experienced a traumatic event related to the performance of their function. The employer designs a regulation for this in consultation with the works council."

    [
      "Travel expenses commuting (expires as of January 1, 2012): The employee receives a monthly reimbursement for the costs of daily commuting between home and work. The employee is free to choose the mode of transport. Costs include public transport (lowest class, cheapest fare) and bridge, tunnel, or ferry fees. Students with a training/employment contract also receive reimbursement when visiting the training school. Reimbursement also applies to costs from: traveling more than once a day due to a split shift with an interruption longer than 2 hours; a call-out for on-call or call-out service; overtime during hours not connected to normal working hours; standby duty during hours not connected to normal working hours. The maximum reimbursement is € 128.25 per month, with an employee contribution of € 67.75 (as of January 1, 2011). This is adjusted with NS fare changes. For private transport, reimbursement is based on the lowest class train fare for the distance. Part-time employees receive pro-rata reimbursement and reduced own contribution. For costs from Article 12.1.1 paragraph 4: public transport (lowest class) costs are reimbursed; for private car use, € 0.30 per kilometer is reimbursed for call-out for on-call, call-out, or overtime. No own contribution or maximum applies. Employees must provide documentation upon request."

They are also prohibited from accepting inheritances from patients who were under treatment in the institution, unless they are a blood or affinity relative up to the fourth degree, spouse, or partner of the employee.     [
      "Social guidance for organizational changes: This chapter provides a framework for organizational changes, with a minimum character allowing deviations in favor of the employee. The employer must balance social, organizational, and economic factors, aiming for job retention. An 'organizational change' is defined as a change in the organization based on a coherent plan with social consequences, including reorganization, merger, collaboration, transfer of undertaking, and full or partial closure. A 'staffing plan' is an indicative overview of functions, including job requirements, expected level, and content. If an organizational change affects 20 or more employees, the employer must inform all employee organizations in writing and invite them for consultation on a social plan. Multiple related changes within three months affecting 20 or more employees are considered a single organizational change. The consultation is held with all CAO-party employee organizations, unless they decline or do not respond. Information provided includes the background and motives for the change, economic and social consequences, current and new organizational structures, the staffing plan, and the impact of contracts with care insurers. An advisory committee for social guidance is established, composed of employer and Works Council members, with a joint chair. This committee advises on the application of this chapter and the social plan, particularly in cases of disagreement regarding non-active status, dismissal, function changes, or hardship clauses. The committee's advice is considered weighty. The employer must also inform and consult employee organizations if there is an intention to establish a new legal entity or transfer part of the organization (affecting 20 or more employees), aiming for a social transfer protocol. Similar consultation is required if an employer manages an entity not under the CAO but partially applying it, and intends to change the legal status of employees, to mitigate social consequences."

      "Year-end bonus: The year-end bonus is 7.33% in 2011, 7.83% in 2012, and 8.33% in 2013. This means that by the end of this CAO's term, the year-end bonus will have reached the level of a full thirteenth month. For employees who have been employed for a full year on December 31, the bonus is 7.33% (2011), 7.83% (2012), or 8.33% (2013) of the annual salary (defined as twelve times the salary applicable on December 1). For employees who have been employed for only part of the period or worked part-time, the bonus is granted pro-rata. If an employee leaves service before the payment date, the year-end bonus is paid pro-rata based on the monthly salary at the end of employment and the number of months the employment contract lasted after December. Payment occurs in December, except for early leavers."

      "Holiday allowance: The holiday allowance is 8% of the annual salary (defined as twelve times the salary applicable on May 1) for employees who have been employed for a full year on May 31. For employees aged 22 or older, the minimum holiday allowance for a full year of employment is €1,699 (per July 1, 2011), €1,703 (per October 1, 2011), €1,729 (per July 1, 2012), or €1,763 (per July 1, 2013). This minimum amount is increased in May of the payment year by any general wage adjustments and the effect of the holiday allowance percentage. For employees who have been employed for only part of the period or worked part-time, the allowance is granted pro-rata. If an employee leaves service before the payment date, the holiday allowance is paid pro-rata based on the monthly salary at the end of employment and the number of months the employment contract lasted after May. Payment occurs in May, except for early leavers. The employer can agree with the Works Council for monthly payment of the holiday allowance, in which case it is 8% of the monthly salary."

The periodic date changes as a result of a promotion or after obtaining a diploma for a first care education. If a personnel assessment system based on the principles in the Social Policy Statute (Appendix A of CAO Ziekenhuizen 1999-2001) provides a reason, the employer can decide not to grant a salary increase in a given year, or to grant it at multiple times within that year."
    ],

The old salary is increased by two periodic increments, after which placement in the higher scale occurs. If granting two periodic increments is not possible due to reaching the scale maximum, the scale is extended with the necessary placement table numbers, after which placement in the higher scale occurs. The new salary cannot exceed the maximum of the scale belonging to the higher function. The employer can agree on a different arrangement with the Works Council."

      "- Acting allowance: An employee who, at the employer's request, fully or almost fully acts in the function of an employee classified in a higher function group receives an allowance. If the acting covers at least half of the daily working hours, the pro-rata principle applies to the allowance. The amount of the acting allowance is equal to the difference between the current salary and the starting salary of the higher-classified function, with a minimum of two periodic increments. Conditions include at least three years of continuous irregular service, no temporary termination or reduction of irregular service, and a difference between the old and new salary (including irregular service allowance) greater than 0.5% of the old salary. The compensation is calculated based on the difference between the old salary (plus the average monthly irregular service allowance over the preceding twelve months) and the new salary (plus any average monthly irregular service allowance to be received over the three months following the change). The compensation amounts to 75% during the first year, 50% during the second year, and 25% during the third year."

Exclusions: a legal entity that operates an academic hospital (as defined in Article 1.2.1 of the WTZi) for higher and scientific education; the Central Military Hospital; the Military Rehabilitation Center in Doorn; a legal entity that exclusively or predominantly offers mental health or addiction care; an organizational unit of a legal entity that applies the CAO Ambulancezorg to employees of that unit. The provisions laid down in a professional statute/code of conduct, as formulated per professional group and ratified by CAO parties, shall be observed. Nevertheless, the employee has the right to refuse to carry out certain assignments on the grounds of serious conscientious objections. In this case, the management ensures that measures are taken within the employee's department to enable this right to be exercised. This obligation is laid down in Article 272 of the Criminal Code and also applies after the termination of employment. The confidentiality obligation does not apply to those who are directly involved or involved as a substitute in the implementation of the treatment agreement (WGBO), the treatment plan (BOPZ), or the research protocol (WMO), insofar as the provision is necessary for the work they perform in that context. The employer is obliged to maintain confidentiality regarding what is known to them concerning the person of the employee by virtue of their function, unless the employee gives permission for the provision of data relating to their person."

      "Work and Rest Time Pattern (Article 6.2): The net number of hours to be worked annually is determined by reducing the agreed working hours (gross) by vacation hours, paid leave, and holiday and commemorative day hours (Article 6.4). The net number of hours to be worked, increased by vacation hours, is recorded in an individual work and rest time pattern. These hours are scheduled on (contractually) agreed days, respecting agreements made before January 1, 2002. The arrangement of the work pattern is determined in consultation with the employee. The consultation takes place at least annually and aims to reach agreement. The employer, insofar as can reasonably be expected, takes into account the employee's personal circumstances outside of work when determining the work schedule, including in any case care (tasks) for children, (dependent) family members, relatives, and close relations, as well as social responsibilities borne by the employee. If individual wishes do not align with the institutional interest, the institutional interest prevails. The distribution of the number of hours to be worked over the year should lead to a balanced work pattern. Both the hours to be worked and the clustered free time must be clearly recognizable in the work pattern. Clustered free time means 56 hours free from duty. An agreed work pattern can be adjusted in consultation. Only at the employee's request can a five-day work week of 7.2 hours per day be adopted. The salary remains based on the agreed average working hours and is therefore decoupled from the actual number of hours worked per month. The employer must enable the employee to work the number of hours established within the annual hours system. If this is not the case, no vacation hours may be deducted. At the end of the employment contract, too many or too few hours worked are corrected as much as possible within the notice period. The remaining difference is paid out or settled with vacation hours or deducted from the last salary."

      "Social Guidance Advisory Committee (Article 15.5): The employer establishes a bipartite advisory committee. Members are appointed by the employer and the works council. The committee draws up regulations governing its composition and working methods. After the regulations are established, the employer and works council receive a copy. The committee advises solely at the request of the employer or an employee on the application of the provisions in this chapter, or the social plan. The employer requests advice from the committee when the employee and employer disagree on the following proposed measures: placing an employee on non-active status; dismissing an employee; a decision regarding a function change; a hardship clause not being honored by an employee. The committee's advice is weighty. The committee periodically reports anonymously on its activities to the employer and the works council. If a social plan applies, this report is also provided to the employee organizations. The costs of the committee are borne by the employer."

      "Statuut Sociaal Beleid (Appendix A): The Social Policy Statute is a guideline for the social policy in institutions, which should be dynamic and develop procedurally. It is an integrated part of the institution's overall policy. Objectives include: creating conditions for employees to contribute; organizing work with maximum autonomy; involving employees in policy; creating conditions for employee development; balancing organizational demands with employee well-being, safety, and health; emphasizing training and development; and ensuring policy is based on justice, legal certainty, and efficiency. Key areas of attention: personnel planning (adequate planning, formation plan, anticipating developments, career planning); personnel provision (stimulating part-time work, recruitment/selection according to NVP code, good introduction, employee files, guidance for end of employment due to disability/pension); career development (opportunities for optimal contribution, interest surveys, internal applications, removing barriers for women in higher positions); quality (recommendation for a Nursing Advisory Council (VAR)); workload management (system to balance care demand and supply); in-house/external professional training, retraining, and further training (all activities for knowledge/skills, employer promotes, creating good learning/work situations for students/employees, interns are supernumerary and focus on learning goals, practical guidance); development, management, and cadre training (operationalizing learning, influencing leadership, function valuation, performance review, work consultation, patient/family relations); change processes (good guidance, good labor relations, efficient organization); work consultation (increasing employee involvement, regular and integrated, not optional, influencing policy on work structure/division/organization/methods/tools, objectives/norms, social relations, working conditions/safety/health)."

    [
      "Overview Norms Arbeidstijdenwet (Atw) and Arbeidstijdenbesluit (Atb) (Appendix C): The Atw applies to all persons working under the employer's authority (employees, students, interns). The Atw and Atb are partly not applicable to employees earning more than three times the minimum wage, unless they perform night work or work involving serious safety or health risks, in which case the Atw applies in full. Additionally, medical specialists are partly exempted. For both aforementioned exceptions, Articles 4:2 and 4:3 (notification and registration), Chapter 5 (working and rest times), and Chapter 6 (co-determination aspects) of the Atw do not apply. Stricter norms apply during pregnancy and after childbirth (Articles 4:5 to 4:9 Atw). This overview only includes norms for employees aged 18 and older. Stricter rules apply to employees younger than 18."

    [
      "Reglement Interpretatiecommissie (Appendix E): The Interpretation Committee's task is to interpret CAO articles based on the negotiations and the parties' intentions. It consists of eight members and an equal number of alternates, with half appointed by each party. The committee hears all matters submitted to it in writing by one of the parties regarding further interpretation of the CAO and issues a ruling. A majority of members must be present for a ruling. If no ruling can be reached, the matter is referred to the CAO parties for a resolution. The committee is chaired by a representative of the party not submitting the question, and the secretariat is handled by NVZ. Meetings are held as often as requested by both chairs or four members, within fourteen days. Meeting invitations and agendas are sent at least seven days in advance (excluding weekends/holidays), which can be shortened in urgent cases. Rulings are made as soon as possible, but no later than three months, and can involve calling experts. Rulings are considered for the next CAO."
    ],
    [
      "Model Employment Contracts (Appendix F): This appendix contains model individual employment contracts for the CAO Ziekenhuizen, including a model individual employment contract, a model learning agreement for the vocational preparatory period, and a model learning/employment agreement. These models specify details such as employer/employee information, function, contract duration, probation period, working hours, salary, vacation, pension, health insurance, notice period, dispute resolution, relocation obligations, and professional matters. They also include a declaration that the CAO and its regulations form an integral part of the agreement, and that the employee has received copies of relevant documents."
    ],
    [
      "Training Agreement (Opleidingsovereenkomst) (Page 98): In cases where the trainer or training institution cannot be bound by the CAO, the institution commits to promoting a training agreement between the institution, the student, and the trainer/training institution. This applies to vocational training (BBL/HBO-V) and specialist training (medical specialists). The agreement must include provisions on: the obligation of the trainer/institution to train the student; the obligation of the student to perform training-related assignments; the start and end of the training agreement; the grounds and method for terminating the training agreement; the dispute resolution for the training; ensuring the training agreement does not conflict with the employment contract; the relationship between the termination of the training agreement and the employment contract; the responsibility and liability of the student and the trainer/institution; the relationship between work and study (if not regulated elsewhere); the costs of the training; and a clause stating that the training agreement, once established, forms an integral part of the employment contract."
