    [
      "Wage Payment during Illness (Article 4.2): The employee who is prevented from performing work due to incapacity for work and has immediately informed the employer thereof, is entitled under the Civil Code to 70% of the wage fixed by time for a maximum period of 104 weeks. The wage fixed by time is understood to mean the employee's salary increased by structural wage components such as irregularity allowance and on-call duty allowance. The structural wage components are measured on a monthly basis over a period of six months preceding the month in which the incapacity for work arose. The employee's wage under paragraph 1 will be supplemented to 100% for the first 52 weeks of incapacity for work, starting from January 1, 2005. For the subsequent 52 weeks, the employee receives 70% of the wage fixed by time, but at least the statutory minimum wage applicable to them. For the hours in which the employee performs suitable work or work without wage value according to their reintegration plan, they receive 100% of the wage fixed by time. Work without wage value is understood to mean performing work on a work-therapeutic basis, attending training, and doing an internship. The wage established under paragraph 1 is adjusted for: general wage increases agreed during the period of incapacity for work; contract changes that were agreed upon before the incapacity for work if the period between the first day of incapacity for work and the effective date of the contract change is less than three months (adjustment takes place from the effective date of the contract change); annual periodic salary increases, provided the employee has not been incapacitated for work for more than 52 weeks. After 52 weeks, the periodic increase is granted for the hours in which the employee is capable of working. The wage is reduced by the amount of the benefit the employee receives under any statutory regulation or an equivalent regulation. In cases where the employer believes that the incapacitated employee has an occupational or chronic illness and/or a life-threatening condition, the employer may provide a wage supplement of a determined amount and duration to this employee."
    ],
    [
      "WIA Supplement (Article 4.7): For the employee who is less than 35% incapacitated for work, the employment relationship is in principle maintained, and incapacity for work itself will not be a reason for dismissal. The starting point for the suitable or adapted function offered by the employer will be that this function is remunerated at least 70% of the remuneration of the previously held function before incapacity for work occurred. The employee who earns less than 50% of their remaining earning capacity with their employer and receives a WGA follow-up benefit under the WIA, is entitled to a supplement to their wage if their income is lower than the social minimum applicable to them. This supplement aims to prevent the employee from having to rely on the Wet Werk en Bijstand for their livelihood. The income referred to in the first sentence is understood to mean the total of wage, WGA follow-up benefit, disability pension, any other benefits, and a supplement under the Toeslagenwet. The amount of the supplement is the difference between their income and the social minimum, but not more than the difference between their income and the wage they earned with this employer before the start of their WGA benefit."

      "Application of Salary Scales (Article 7.1.5) (expires January 1, 2014): For the application of the functional salary scale, the employee must have the number of function years mentioned below: for functional salary scales 16 to 20: one function year; for functional salary scales 21 to 26: two function years; for functional salary scales 27 to 40: three function years. Employees who do not have the required number of function years are classified in the entry salary scale belonging to the functional salary scale. With effect from January 1, 2013, all entry scales have been abolished, and thus the application of paragraphs 1 and 3. Employees younger than 22 years are classified in the youth scale belonging to the functional salary scale. With effect from January 1, 2012, the youth scales are phased out as follows: with effect from January 1, 2012, all scales for 21-year-olds are abolished; with effect from January 1, 2013, all scales for 20-year-olds are abolished; with effect from January 1, 2014, all scales for 17, 18, and 19-year-olds are abolished. With effect from January 1, 2014, all youth scales have been abolished, and thus the application of paragraph 2. The employer may deviate from the number of prescribed function years as referred to in paragraph 1. If the function performance gives cause to do so, the entry scale may be applied for a longer period. The employer communicates this in writing and with reasons to the employee."

    [
      "Reclassification of Already Classified Functions (Article 7.1.6): A reclassification of a function takes place in accordance with Articles 2.2 to 2.5 of the Protocol FunctieWaardering Gezondheidszorg®. If, as a result of the reclassification decision, a lower function group becomes applicable to the employee, they can reach the maximum salary as laid down in the employment contract, provided this does not exceed the maximum salary plus 10% belonging to the lower function group. If the employee's salary is more than the maximum salary of the lower function group increased by 10%, the salary as laid down in the employment contract is frozen. The ultimately reached or frozen salary as referred to in paragraph 3 is adjusted with the general wage adjustments as applicable for this CAO. If, as a result of the reclassification decision, a higher function group becomes applicable to the employee, the salary scales belonging to the higher function group apply. The outcome of a reclassification procedure is retroactive to the moment when agreement exists between employer and employee on the job description in the reclassification procedure. When classifying into the new salary scale, at least the amount corresponding to the old salary applies. If this amount is not present in the new salary scale, the next higher amount in that salary scale applies. A reclassification into a higher function group is not a promotion in the sense of Article 7.1.9. A re-description of a function takes place in accordance with Articles 2.6 to 2.7 of the Protocol FunctieWaardering Gezondheidszorg® (Appendix D)."
    ],
    [
      "Periodic Increases (Article 7.1.7): Unless otherwise stipulated in the employment contract, a salary increase within the entry or functional scale is granted once a year. The periodic date is, with the exception of the provisions in paragraphs 2, 3, and 4, the date of commencement of employment. If employment commences during the calendar month, the periodic date is the first of the month following the month of commencement of employment. For employees classified in the youth scale at the start of employment, the periodic date is the first of the month in which the person celebrates their birthday. Upon transfer to the functional scale, the periodic date does not change. With effect from January 1, 2014, all youth scales have been abolished, and thus the application of this paragraph. Every periodic date changes as a result of a promotion and after obtaining a diploma for a first care training: in case of promotion, the promotion date becomes the new periodic date; in case of obtaining a diploma, the periodic date is the first of the month after obtaining the diploma. If the application of a personnel appraisal system based on the principles included in the Social Policy Statute (Appendix A of CAO Ziekenhuizen 1999-2001) gives cause to do so, the employer may decide not to grant a salary increase in any given year or to grant it at multiple times in that year."
    ],
    [
      "Gratuity and Allowances (Article 7.1.8): The employer may grant a gratuity or temporary allowance based on function performance or the labor market situation. Gratuities and temporary allowances do not have a structural character and are not included in the pensionable salary."
    ],
    [
      "Promotion (Article 7.1.9): In case of promotion to a function classified in a higher function group, the new salary is based on the salary scale belonging to the higher function group. The old salary is increased by two periodic increments, after which classification into the higher scale takes place. If granting two periodic increments is not possible due to reaching the scale maximum, the scale is extended with the necessary classification table numbers, after which classification into the higher scale takes place. The new salary may not exceed the maximum of the scale belonging to the higher function. The employer may make a deviating arrangement in consultation with the works council."
    ],
    [
      "Acting Allowance (Article 7.1.10): The employee who, at the employer's request, fully or almost fully acts in the function of an employee classified in a higher function group, receives compensation for this. If acting takes place for at least half of the daily working hours, the pro-rata principle applies to the compensation. The amount of the acting allowance is equal to the difference between the current salary and the starting salary of the higher classified function, with a minimum of two periodic increments. If granting two periodic increments is not possible due to reaching the scale maximum, the scale is extended with the necessary classification table numbers. The salary, including the allowance, cannot exceed the maximum of the scale of the function being acted in. Acting due to vacation or for less than one month is not eligible for compensation. The employer may make a deviating arrangement in consultation with the works council. The acting allowance does not have a structural character and is not included in the pensionable salary."
    ],

If the contractual working hours have changed in the relevant service years, the salary is increased or decreased proportionally."

      "Gratuity at End of Employment (Article 7.1.12): At the moment the employment contract ends due to reaching the AOW/old-age pension age, the employee is entitled to a gratuity equal to half of the salary. At the moment the employment contract ends due to the use of the bridging benefit (OBU) or Flex-pension from the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn scheme, the employee is entitled to a gratuity equal to half of the salary. The payment takes place if no new employment is entered into. If the employee enters into an employment relationship with the same employer in addition to the OBU, the gratuity referred to in paragraph 1 will be granted at the end of the new employment contract. The amount of the gratuity is based on the working hours and the IP number on the day preceding the use of the OBU. If the employee uses the FLEX-pension and remains employed, the gratuity referred to in paragraph 1 will be granted at the end of the employment contract. The amount of the gratuity is based on the working hours and the IP number on the day preceding the use of the FLEX-pension. If the employee uses the exchange-OBU, the gratuity referred to in paragraph 1 will be granted at the moment the employee goes on full OBU and no new employment is entered into. The amount of the gratuity is based on the working hours and the IP number on the day preceding the use of the exchange-OBU."

The sterilization assistant student and the BBL student level 1 and 2 receive the salary stated in Appendix 1 of this chapter, insofar as the minimum (youth) wage is not higher. The condition is that the internship amounts to at least 144 hours per academic year. The internship allowance is not granted if a BBL student has an employment contract that entitles them to wage payment during the internship period. Students within the meaning of this article are MBO and HBO students. The amount of the internship allowance for a full-time internship is €308 per month (January 1, 2011). For a part-time intern, the pro-rata principle applies. The internship allowance is indexed annually with the consumer price index. The internship allowance includes the intern's incurred costs, with the exception of the allowance as referred to in Article 12.1.1 paragraph 5, as it reads from January 1, 2012."

      "End-of-Year Bonus (Article 7.4.1): The end-of-year bonus for employees who have been employed for a full year on December 31, 2011, is 7.33% of the annual salary. If the employee has been employed for only part of the period over which the end-of-year bonus is calculated and/or has worked part-time during that period or part thereof, they are entitled to a pro-rata end-of-year bonus. For this purpose, annual salary is understood to mean twelve times the salary applicable on May 1. For employees aged 22 or older, the holiday allowance for a full year of employment in any given year is at least €1,699 (as of July 1, 2011), €1,703 (as of October 1, 2011), €1,729 (as of July 1, 2012), or €1,763 (as of July 1, 2013). The minimum amount is increased in May of the payment year by: any general wage adjustments that have taken place within the framework of the CAO Ziekenhuizen; the effect of any increase in the holiday allowance percentage. If the employee has been employed for only part of the period over which the holiday allowance is calculated and/or has worked part-time during that period or part thereof, they are entitled to a pro-rata holiday allowance. If the employee leaves employment before the payment date, the holiday allowance is paid pro-rata based on the monthly salary applicable at the end of employment, for the number of months the employment contract lasted after May. Payment of the holiday allowance takes place in May, with the exception of the situation mentioned in paragraph 4. The employer may, in consultation with the works council, make a deviating arrangement for employees who have indicated that they wish to receive their holiday allowance monthly.       "Monthly salary = hourly salary x 45 x 52 / 12. Note: Due to rounding to whole euros, the monthly salary deviates slightly from the salary obtained by multiplying the hourly rate by the agreed number of hours per week, then multiplying by 52 weeks, and dividing the result by 12 months. The starting point, however, is the monthly salary in euros. For the determination of the hourly rate based on this calculation, 2340 hours per year have been assumed. Any additional hours per year due to a year having 365 days or in the case of a leap year are deemed to belong to the agreed working hours and to be included in the monthly salary. The monthly amounts are rounded to whole euros."

  "pension_information": [
    [
      "Pension Fund Zorg en Welzijn (Article 3.3.2): Article 3.3.2 paragraph 3 is amended as follows: 'The pension premium is recovered for 50% from the employee.' In principle, all employees working on the basis of an employment contract are compulsorily insured for their pension with the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn. The scope, as well as the rights and obligations of the employer and employee, with regard to the pension, are regulated in the statutes and the pension regulations."
    ],
    [
      "Pension during Incapacity for Work (Article 4.6): From the second year of illness, the employee can voluntarily continue to accrue pension up to a maximum of the level applicable on the last day of the first year of illness. The employee receives the determined employer's contribution to the pension premium based on the chosen level of continuation."
    ],
    [
      "Premiums during Unpaid Leave (Article 13.3.2): Under the conditions included in the pension regulations, the employee may voluntarily continue pension insurance during the period of extended maternity leave, parental leave, and life-course leave. The employee receives, notwithstanding paragraph 1, the determined employer's contribution to the pension premium based on the chosen level of continuation. Paragraph 2 is only applicable if the employee continues employment for at least six months after the unpaid leave. If employment ends within this period, the employer's share of the premiums during the unpaid leave period will be repaid by the employee."
    ],
    [
      "Waiting Allowance and Pension (Article 14.5): During the waiting allowance period, if and as long as the waiting allowance recipient wishes to continue participation in Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn, pension entitlements remain related to the salary, being the contribution basis under the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn Regulations. The employer is responsible for the payment of the premium due. The employee's share of the waiting allowance recipient is related to the amount of the waiting allowance, reduced by the allowances included in the waiting allowance that are not included in the contribution basis. If and as long as the Pension Accrual during Unemployment Scheme (FVP) applies to the waiting allowance recipient, the provisions in paragraph 1 regarding the premium due do not apply to the waiting allowance recipient. The provisions in paragraph 1 do not apply if the waiting allowance recipient who is younger than fifty years old can participate in the pension scheme associated with their potential new employment. In the event that the employer's supplement is not sufficient to cover the premium due by the waiting allowance recipient, the person concerned must pay the missing part from the statutory benefit. The premium due on income above the contribution limit mentioned in the Pension Accrual during Unemployment Scheme is borne by the employer, after deduction of the proportional employee's share."

Paragraph 3.3 Allowances and Gratuity indicates which allowances are not calculated as pensionable income. The CAO agreements regarding the employer-employee premium distribution are applicable."

  "leave_information": [
    [
      "Personal Life-Phase Budget (PLB): The personal life-phase budget (PLB) in time was introduced on January 1, 2010, for all employees covered by the CAO Ziekenhuizen. This PLB is used for spending goals in time. The purpose of the scheme is to give employees the opportunity to use a saved budget when their life phase gives cause to do so. Social partners aim to promote the sustainable employability of employees. In 2012, annually and for the first time in December, at the employee's request, a maximum of 22 hours of the PLB budget can be used in the multiple-choice system of employment conditions (MKSA). Part-time employees who work extra hours generally receive PLB time for the extra hours in the form of a surcharge on their hourly wage. During incapacity for work/illness, the employee accrues their PLB budget for a maximum period of six months. 'A period of maximum six months' refers to the first six months after reporting sick. After that, no further accrual takes place for the incapacitated hours. For the purpose of determining the six months, periods are aggregated if they follow each other with an interruption of less than one month. To transfer PLB hours to another institution applying the CAO Ziekenhuizen, the employee must have at least 50 PLB hours, or the transfer must relate to a minimum of 50 PLB hours. For employees who do not have 50 PLB hours, the employer will, upon receipt of the employee's resignation, draw the employee's attention to the consequences of the resignation for the PLB budget. With effect from January 1, 2011, accrued PLB leave can no longer be fully taken prior to the pensionable age. The annual working hours may be reduced by a maximum of 50% compared to the annual working hours in the preceding calendar year. This condition does not apply to employees who, due to compelling business interests, could not use PLB hours during their employment. The current above-statutory vacation hours will be abolished with effect from January 1, 2012. These 22 hours will be added to the employee's applicable PLB budget. With regard to statutory vacation hours, the Civil Code is followed."
    ],
    [
      "Pregnancy and Maternity Leave (Article 13.4.2): Article 13.4.2 paragraph 1 is amended as follows: 'The employee is entitled to a benefit from the UWV during the period of pregnancy and maternity leave. The employer supplements the benefit to the wage fixed by time for the employee.'"
    ],
    [
      "Calamity Leave (Article 13.4.5): Article 13.4.5 paragraph 2 is amended as follows: 'In case of death of blood relatives and relatives by marriage in the direct line - a brother or sister of the employee and/or of their spouse or partner, the employee is entitled to paid leave on the day of death and on the day of the funeral or cremation, insofar as the employee was scheduled to work on this day/these days. In case of death of the spouse or partner - a (foster) child - a (foster) parent of the employee and/or of their spouse or partner, the employee is entitled to paid leave from the day of death up to and including the day of the funeral or cremation, insofar as the employee was scheduled to work on this day/these days.'"
    ],
    [
      "Holidays and Commemorative Days (Article 1.1.1.o): New Year's Day, first and second Easter Day, Ascension Day, first and second Pentecost Day, first and second Christmas Day, and national holidays and commemorative days recognized as such by the government, including Queen's Day and Liberation Day each year, as well as special holidays and commemorative days determined by the employer in consultation with the works council (such as the institution's X-year jubilee)."
    ],
    [
      "Annual Hours System and Vacation (Article 6.3): If vacation hours are taken on a day on which a certain number of hours would have been worked according to the work and rest time pattern, this number of hours is registered as vacation."
    ],
    [
      "Annual Hours System and Holidays and Commemorative Days (Article 6.4): The number of hours to be worked annually is reduced by 7.2 hours for each holiday and commemorative day not falling on a Saturday or Sunday. For employees with a working week deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies with regard to deployability on holidays and with regard to the provisions in paragraph 1. For employees who commence employment during the calendar year, the reduction applies based on the holidays occurring in the remaining part of the calendar year. If desired, the employee may exchange holidays and commemorative days for the holidays and commemorative days applicable to their belief. The employer may, in consultation with the works council, make a deviating arrangement. This arrangement replaces the provisions in this article. If no agreement is reached, the provisions in this article remain applicable."
    ],
    [
      "Annual Hours System and Illness/Incapacity for Work (Article 6.5): If the employee does not perform their work due to illness/incapacity for work, the number of registered sick hours is based on the agreed work pattern. If no work pattern has been agreed, the average contractual working hours apply. If the employee does not perform their work due to pregnancy and maternity leave, the average contractual working hours apply."
    ],
    [
      "Paid Leave in connection with Membership of Employee Organizations (Article 5.3): The employer enables the employee to participate in activities of the employee organization of which they are a member. The employee receives paid leave up to a total of 228 hours per year, if these activities take place during hours when they are available according to the employment contract. For employees with a part-time working week, the pro-rata principle is not applied. Employee organizations as referred to in paragraph 1 include: an employee organization that is a party to this CAO; a central federation to which an employee organization that is a party to this CAO is affiliated; professional organizations affiliated with an employee organization that is a party to this CAO. This explicitly excludes the professional organizations mentioned in the preamble to this CAO under II the following organizations of employees: the Federation of Social Work and Welfare; the Federation for Psychodiagnostic Workers. Activities as referred to in paragraph 1 include: statutory meetings, as well as meetings of statutory regional organizations, insofar as the employee is designated as a board member and/or delegate; conferences, national and regional meetings, and working groups, insofar as the employee has been invited by the main board; courses, insofar as the employee participates or has been invited by the main board. Insofar as it concerns activities of (professional) associations as referred to in paragraph 3, only activities related to collective employment conditions are included under the aforementioned activities. For employees who are union consultants in an institution with 1500 employees or more, an exemption of eight hours per week applies. For union consultants in an institution with at least five hundred but less than 1500 employees, an exemption of four hours per week applies. A maximum of two union consultants (one per employee organization) can be exempted per institution."
    ],
    [
      "Other Paid Leave (Article 5.4): The employer enables employees who are board members of a professional association to participate in activities of the professional organization of which they are a member. Professional organizations are understood to mean the professional associations mentioned in the preamble to this CAO under II the following organizations of employees. Activities are understood to mean meetings and work performed in special committees and related to professional content. The employee receives paid leave up to a total of 36 hours per year, if these activities take place during hours when they are available according to the employment contract. For employees with a working week deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle is not applied."
    ],
    [
      "Vacation (Article 13.1.1) (expires January 1, 2012): For the purpose of determining working hours in a work schedule, the actual number of vacation hours taken by the employee is counted. If vacation is taken on a day on which a daily working time of nine hours would have been scheduled, then nine vacation hours are counted towards the determination of working hours and similarly for the settlement of the number of vacation hours taken. The result of the calculation of the number of vacation hours applicable to the employee in any given year is rounded up to whole hours. For employees with a working week deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies."
    ],
    [
      "Vacation Hours (Article 13.1.2) (expires January 1, 2012): The employee is entitled to 166 vacation hours per calendar year with retention of salary. Of these, 144 hours are statutory (Article 7:634 BW) and 22 hours are granted as above-statutory. Employees who, before January 1, 2004, made use of Article 6.13 (55-plus scheme) or Article 6.14 (lgpb) of the CAO Ziekenhuizen 2008-2009 are entitled to 184 vacation hours per calendar year instead of 166 hours."
    ],
    [
      "Accrual of Vacation Hours (Article 13.1.3) (expires January 1, 2012): For each calendar month in which the employee is or will be employed, vacation accrues at 1/12th of the annually determined vacation. A month in which employment commenced before the 16th or ended after the 15th is considered a full calendar month for vacation determination. If employment commenced after the 15th of the month or ended before the 16th of the month, vacation rights accrue pro-rata. For employees who have not performed their work due to incapacity for work, the accrual of their vacation rights continues for the last six months of that incapacity for work (Article 7:635 paragraph 4 BW). The provisions in paragraph 2 also apply, in addition to the statutory provisions, to partially incapacitated employees (Article 7:635 BW). The right to accrue vacation rights during pregnancy/maternity leave is granted independently of any preceding or subsequent incapacity for work (Article 7:635 paragraph 2 BW)."
    ],
    [
      "Designating Vacation Days (Article 13.1.4) (expires January 1, 2012): The employer may determine that the employee takes vacation leave on two working days designated by the employer. This leave is included in the number of hours mentioned in Article 13.1.2. This designation takes place: in consultation with the works council; no later than the end of January; for one or more groups of employees."
    ],
    [
      "Taking Vacation Hours (Article 13.1.5) (expires January 1, 2012): Vacation must be taken in the calendar year in which the entitlement arose. Vacation not taken in the interest of the institution is granted in the following calendar year. This may be deviated from in very exceptional cases. For taking vacation hours during partial incapacity for work, the number of hours to which the employee is entitled during their partial incapacity for work can only be taken for the part in which the person is working. Vacation is granted, unless the interests of the department or service where the employee is employed prevent it, in accordance with the employee's wishes - uninterrupted if desired. The employee can claim at least three consecutive weeks of vacation, including the weekends preceding and following it. The employer may make an arrangement that allows a maximum of three sick days to be designated as vacation leave if the employee takes vacation during the incapacity for work period with the company doctor's permission, provided that the employee retains the right to at least the number of vacation days mentioned in Article 7:634 BW. The general arrangement and the distribution of vacation within the institution require the consent of the works council."

The employer determines the new vacation period in consultation with the employee. The damage suffered by the employee as a result of this change is compensated by the employer."

      "Personal Life-Phase Budget (PLB) (Article 13.2.1): With effect from January 1, 2011, the employee receives a PLB of thirty hours for that calendar year. From January 1, 2012, the employee annually receives a PLB of 57 hours. Upon commencement and termination of employment within a calendar year, the PLB is granted pro-rata based on the number of months of employment. For employees with a working week deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies."

    [
      "Principles of Personal Life-Phase Budget (Article 13.2.2): The employer manages the PLB administration. Upon changes in the employment percentage and at the end of employment, the employee receives a new overview of their accrued budget. The PLB is primarily intended for spending goals in time. The purpose of the scheme is to enable the employee to use a saved budget when their life phase gives cause to do so. The introduction of PLB hours aims to promote the sustainable employability of the employee. Taking leave can be done at the employee's discretion and wishes, subject to the following provisions: the employee who wishes to take substantial leave must submit a written request to the employer at least four months before the start of the leave, stating the duration and scope of the leave; the four-month period is not applicable if there is no question of a substantial leave; the employer will grant the request for leave unless such a compelling business or service interest opposes the taking of leave that the employee's interest must yield according to standards of reasonableness and fairness. The employee may take PLB leave prior to the pensionable age for a maximum of half of the working hours of the preceding calendar year, unless the employee could not take the remaining leave earlier due to compelling business interests. The employee with a working week deviating from the full-time norm who incidentally works overtime generally receives PLB for the extra hours worked in the form of a surcharge on their hourly wage. The PLB has no expiry date. Annually, and for the first time in December 2012, at the employee's request, a maximum of 22 hours of the PLB can be used in the Multiple Choice System of Employment Conditions (MKSA). For this use, the pro-rata principle does not apply. Upon termination of employment, upon death, and through use in the MKSA, a payout of accrued rights may occur. The total value of the PLB can be (partially) deposited into the life-course scheme upon termination of employment. The value of an accrued hour is the employee's hourly wage at the time of the buy-out. Upon termination of employment, the PLB is transferred to a new hospital employer at the employee's request. The transfer must relate to at least fifty PLB hours. If the employee does not have at least fifty PLB hours, the employer will immediately, upon receipt of the resignation, inform them of the consequences of this provision. The accrual of the PLB continues for a maximum of six months in case of illness/incapacity for work. For the purpose of determining the six months, periods are aggregated if they follow each other with an interruption of less than four weeks. Once accrued leave does not lapse during illness/incapacity for work."

      "Definitions of Leave (Article 13.3.1): In this regulation, paid leave is understood to mean the number of hours to be taken by the employee in a work schedule under this regulation, during which no work needs to be performed. These hours count towards the determination of the total working hours. In this regulation, unpaid leave is understood to mean the right to be free from all duties. Unpaid leave granted under this regulation is disregarded in the determination of the total working hours. Children residing in the employee's household for whom an adoption application has been submitted are considered the employee's child for the purpose of this paragraph. For employees who are members of a religious community, this paragraph applies mutatis mutandis. In that case, a priestly jubilee is equated with a wedding celebration. For employees with a working week deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies."

A request to use the life-course balance for full early retirement will be honored by the employer. If the employee wishes to terminate the leave prematurely, the employer will endeavor to re-employ the employee as soon as possible."

Marriage or registration of partnership of one of the members of the employee's household: one day. 12.5 and 25 years of marriage of the employee: one day. 12.5, 25, 40, 50, 60 years of marriage of parents or foster parents of the employee, of the spouse or partner: one day. 12.5 and 25 years of service jubilee of the employee: one day.       "Savings Leave (Article 8.5): Pursuant to Article 7:634 BW, employees with a full-time employment are entitled to at least 144 vacation hours per year (statutory vacation hours). For employees with a working week deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies (see Article 13.1.1 paragraph 3). Agreements on savings leave can only be made for hours exceeding this statutory minimum (above-statutory vacation hours). Notwithstanding Article 13.1.5 paragraph 1, employer and employee may agree that above-statutory vacation hours can be saved (from January 1, 2012, only applicable to above-statutory vacation hours as referred to in Article 13.1.2 paragraph 2). Career reorientation is understood to mean undertaking activities (e.g., training, courses, internship, study trip) determined in consultation with the employer. Career reorientation does not include preparation for the OBU, or any other provision in the context of pre-retirement. The employee is given the opportunity to extend the leave saved in the agreed period with unpaid leave up to a maximum of six months."

      "Duration of Employment Contract (Article 3.1.2): The employment contract is generally entered into for an indefinite period. This does not apply to the employment contract of employees who have already reached the old-age pensionable age upon commencement of employment. For a fixed-term employment contract, the reason or duration must be stated. Article 7:668a BW applies to a series of fixed-term employment contracts. The effect of Article 7:668a BW paragraph 1 sub b is excluded for temporary agency workers. If the temporary agency worker enters into a fixed-term employment contract with the same institution within three months after the termination of the temporary agency contract, this employment contract is considered a second fixed-term employment contract. Article 7:668a BW paragraph 2 remains applicable. Pursuant to Article 7:668a BW paragraph 5, the employer is permitted to extend a fixed-term contract in the following exhaustively listed situations, without an employment contract for an indefinite period arising: if a fixed-term employment contract as a doctor not in training to become a specialist (anios) ends, a fixed-term employment contract as a doctor in training to become a specialist (aios) or as a doctor in training for a recognized medical follow-up training can be entered into for the duration of the training; if a fixed-term employment contract relating to the performance of research ends, a new fixed-term employment contract relating to the performance of research, with a maximum duration of four years, can be entered into; if a fixed-term employment contract relating to the completion of a training ends, a new employment contract for the remaining duration of the training can be entered into, if the extension is necessary in connection with the completion of the training."

    [
      "Suspension (Article 3.1.4): The employer may suspend the employee for a maximum of one week with retention of salary, for such serious reasons that continuation of the employee's work is no longer deemed responsible by the employer. The employer may extend the suspension once by a maximum of one week. The employer cannot impose an extension simultaneously with the pronouncement of the first suspension. The decision to suspend or the decision to extend it is immediately and with reasons communicated to the employee and confirmed by registered letter. The employee is given the opportunity to account for themselves to the employer within four days of the date of the registered letter (excluding Saturdays, Sundays, and holidays). They may be assisted by a legal counsel. The suspension may be extended until the effective date of the termination of the employment contract when: the employer has immediately initiated a dismissal procedure with the UWV Werk en Inkomen; or a request for dissolution of the employment contract has been submitted to the court or the Scheidsgerecht Gezondheidszorg. During the suspension, the employee retains their salary. The employer is authorized to deny the employee access to the institution's buildings and grounds during the suspension period, insofar as this does not relate to the employee's living space. If it appears that the employee was clearly wrongly suspended by the employer, the employer will, at the employee's request, publicly rehabilitate them and compensate them for demonstrable damage."

    [
      "Non-Active Status (Article 3.1.5): The employer may place the employee on non-active status for a maximum of three weeks if, in the employer's opinion, the progress of the work - for whatever reason - is seriously impeded. The employer may extend the non-active status once by a maximum of three weeks. With the consent of the employee or their representative, another extension of a maximum of three weeks may be agreed upon. The decision to place on non-active status or the decision to extend it is communicated with reasons by the employer to the employee and confirmed in writing as soon as possible. The employer may place the employee on non-active status for the remaining duration of the notice period, after a legally valid notice period has commenced. The condition is that, in the employer's opinion, compelling interests make this necessary. During the non-active status, the employee retains their salary and all other rights arising from the employment contract/CAO. The employer must promote the employee's return to work during the period of non-active status. After the expiry of the non-active status period, the employee is entitled to resume their work. Non-active status cannot be used as a disciplinary measure."

      "Termination (Article 3.1.7): The employment contract is terminated in writing. A fixed-term employment contract can only be terminated prematurely if that right has been agreed upon in writing for each party. An employment contract may not be terminated, with the exception of termination for urgent reasons as referred to in Articles 7:678 BW and 7:679 BW, unless the employer is in possession of a dismissal permit. The party terminating the employment contract provides, upon request, even during an agreed probationary period, written notification of the reason for termination. The effective date of dismissal is the first of the calendar month. The employer cannot terminate during the period that the employee is unfit to perform their work due to illness, unless the unfitness has lasted for two years, or the unfitness arose after a request for a dismissal permit was received by the CWI. The two-year period does not apply in the situations described in Article 7:670b paragraph 3 BW. The employer conducts an exit interview with the employee who wishes to terminate the employment contract by resignation. When the statutory employee's notice period is extended up to and including three months, the employer's notice period may be equated with the employee's notice period. For an extension of the employee's notice period to a period of four, five, or six months, the employer's period is doubled. If the employer terminates the employment contract, the statutory notice period applies if it leads to a longer period than the notice period included in the employment contract.       "Waiting Allowance (Chapter 14): For the application of the further provisions of this chapter, 'Waiting Allowance' is understood to mean: the sum of, on the one hand, a WW benefit, any ZW benefit, a WIA benefit, an OBU benefit from Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn; income from work or business, insofar as Article 14.2 paragraph 2 or Article 14.3 applies, and on the other hand, the employer's supplement thereto.     [
      "Waiting Allowance Scope (Article 14.2): Waiting allowance is granted to employees whose employment contract does not end at their own request, starting from the day following the end of employment, if this termination occurs due to: full or partial abolition of their function or formation place; reorganization resulting in full or partial abolition of their work; merger, liquidation, full or partial closure of the institution; unsuitability of the employee, which is not attributable to their own fault or actions. The employee must have reached the age of fifty and have been employed for fifteen years or longer by the employer or its legal predecessor. The waiting allowance is granted if the employee has been awarded a WW benefit and otherwise does everything necessary to obtain the benefits mentioned in Article 14.1. If the waiting allowance recipient receives a ZW or WIA benefit on the day the waiting allowance would commence, this is supplemented to the level and for the duration of the waiting allowance. If no WW benefit is granted within two years after the aforementioned day, the waiting allowance ends no later than after two years. Employees who are notified in writing by the employer that their employment contract will be terminated on one of the grounds mentioned in paragraph 1, and who accept another employment before the date of dismissal with a lower salary, will receive a supplement to that salary, starting from the date of commencement of employment, up to the level and for the duration of the waiting allowance."
    ],
    [
      "Waiting Allowance Duration (Article 14.3): The waiting allowance is granted for three months, increased by three months for each full year of service, with a maximum of twenty years of service taken into account for the application of this provision. For employees who will reach the pensionable age within five years after the date of dismissal and who, moreover, will have completed at least ten years of service with the institution on the date of dismissal, the duration of the waiting allowance is extended until that age is reached. For employees who will be eligible for the full bridging benefit (OBU) as referred to in the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn Regulations within five years after the date of dismissal, the duration of the waiting allowance is extended until this time, subject to the following conditions: on the date of dismissal, at least ten years of service must have been completed with the institution; during the aforementioned period of a maximum of five years, participation in the bridging benefit scheme under the regulations of the aforementioned Pension Fund must have been voluntarily continued. During the extended waiting allowance period mentioned in paragraphs 2 and 3, the provisions of Article 14.4 paragraph 1 apply. For the purpose of determining the number of years of service as referred to in paragraph 1, all full months continuously spent in the service of institutions affiliated with the NVZ, NZf, or their legal predecessors count. Years of service spent in the aforementioned institutions, which are not continuously consecutive and during which interruptions the employee received waiting allowance under this CAO, are deemed to be consecutive for the purpose of this provision. If the entitlement to waiting allowance has ended because the employee receives income from employment and this employment does not end at their own request for a reason mentioned in Article 14.2 paragraph 1, the entitlement to waiting allowance revives. The duration of the employment is deducted from the total duration of the waiting allowance entitlement. The revival of waiting allowance does not apply if and as long as there is an entitlement to waiting allowance with the new employer."
    ],
    [
      "Waiting Allowance Amount (Article 14.4): The waiting allowance is equal to the last earned salary for the first six months, and subsequently amounts to 80% for the next three months, 75% for the following two years, and then 70% of the salary. During the waiting allowance period, the waiting allowance is never less than 80% of the minimum wage as referred to in the Wet minimumloon en minimum vakantiebijslag. General wage adjustments as applicable for this CAO, which the waiting allowance recipient would have received if they had remained employed, will be taken into account in the calculation of the waiting allowance. In that case, the prevailing premium rates at that time will be observed for the calculation of the net income. If, as a result of the gradual or non-gradual reduction of individual work, the application of the provisions in the preceding paragraphs would lead to unfavorable results for the waiting allowance recipient, those provisions will be deviated from in their favor."
    ],

      "Waiting Allowance Recipient Obligations (Article 14.6): The employee is obliged, after notification of dismissal, to immediately register as a job seeker with the Employment Office. The employee/waiting allowance recipient is obliged to make use of any opportunity offered to obtain income from work or business, unless they demonstrate that this obligation cannot reasonably be expected of them. The employee/waiting allowance recipient is obliged to immediately report to the employer the amount of income from work or business and the amount of any benefit received under a statutory regulation. If requested, they must provide all desired information and supporting documents. The employee/waiting allowance recipient who is eligible for the Pension Accrual during Unemployment Scheme is obliged to cooperate with the continuation of pension accrual, as stipulated in this scheme."
    ],
    [
      "Reduction of Waiting Allowance (Article 14.7): If the waiting allowance recipient receives income from work or business on or after the day the waiting allowance commenced, this income will be deducted from the waiting allowance if and insofar as it, together with the waiting allowance, exceeds the last earned salary. If a penalty reduction is applied to an employee's WW benefit, the employer's obligation to supplement as referred to in Article 14.4 will be adjusted accordingly, and the employee cannot invoke wage payment under Article 7:629 BW. If the income from business or work as referred to in paragraph 1 leads to a (partial) reduction of the statutory benefit, the part of the income that is deducted from the statutory benefit will become part of the waiting allowance."
    ],
    [
      "Cessation of Waiting Allowance (Article 14.8): The waiting allowance ceases: with effect from the day following the day on which the employee dies; with effect from the day on which the employee becomes entitled to an old-age or disability pension under the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn Regulations, by virtue of the employment from which they were dismissed with waiting allowance; if the waiting allowance recipient fails to comply with the obligations mentioned in Article 14.6; if one of the benefits mentioned in Article 14.1 ceases, because the waiting allowance recipient does not do everything necessary to obtain one of these benefits. Pending an appeal procedure under the aforementioned statutory regulations, the payment of the waiting allowance is suspended. The waiting allowance may be declared forfeited by the employer if: the waiting allowance recipient fails to cooperate with a medical examination aimed at applying for a disability pension or a statutory benefit due to incapacity for work; the waiting allowance recipient resides permanently abroad. This provision does not apply to persons who have reached the age of 55."
    ],
    [
      "Guarantee of Waiting Allowance (Article 14.12): The employer is obliged, in the event of the closure of the institution, to take timely measures that guarantee the payment of the waiting allowance."
    ],
    [
      "Social Plan (Article 15.4): The employer draws up a social plan in consultation with the employee organizations. The social plan aims to limit the negative social consequences for employees due to an organizational change as much as possible. The social plan addresses, among other things: redeployment, possibly in combination with temporary placement or secondment; the prevention of involuntary dismissal through redeployment and natural attrition; training and retraining, both aimed at redeployment and at increasing opportunities in the labor market; outplacement and other forms of support to increase opportunities in the labor market; financial support in cases where involuntary dismissal is unavoidable; a salary guarantee scheme; a hardship clause in cases where the social plan leads to an unreasonable or unfair situation for the individual employee; the period over which these measures extend; the position of on-call employees; the position of possible future self-employed personnel."
    ],
    [
      "Information and Consultation on Transfer and/or Change of Legal Status (Article 15.6): If the employer intends to establish a new legal entity, or if the employer intends to transfer part of the organization to an existing legal entity, involving the transfer of twenty or more employees to this legal entity, the employer will inform all employee organizations as referred to in Article 15.3 paragraph 3 in writing as soon as possible and invite them in good time for consultation. The intention of the consultation is to reach a social transfer protocol. If an employer as referred to in Article 1.1.1.a manages a legal entity that does not fall under the scope of the CAO Ziekenhuizen, but which does (partially) apply the CAO Ziekenhuizen, and intends to change the legal status of employees, the employer will invite the employee organizations referred to in paragraph 1 for consultation. The intention of the consultation is to make agreements to limit the social consequences of the change of legal status for the employees as much as possible."
    ]

  "overtime_information": [
    [
      "Consignation Break in Night Shift (effective January 1, 2012): If the employer uses an on-call break in the night shift, and the employee is therefore not allowed to leave the workplace, they receive a consignation allowance of €5 gross per half-hour break."
    ],
    [
      "Work and Rest Times (Article 6.7): Insofar as this CAO does not provide otherwise, the provisions of the Working Hours Act (Atw) and the Working Hours Decree (Atb) apply. The employer notifies the employee of the work and rest time pattern at least 28 days in advance. If, due to the nature of the work, the work and rest time pattern cannot be communicated to the employee at least 28 days in advance, the employer notifies the employee at least 28 days in advance of the day on which the weekly rest period begins and on which Sundays the employee does not have to perform work. The employer also notifies the employee at least four days in advance of the times at which they must perform work. The arrangement of work and rest times is determined by the employer, with the understanding that working hours preferably fall between 07:00 and 20:00 from Monday to Friday, and between 8:00 and 12:00 on Saturday. If there is a work and rest time arrangement with rotating shifts, these shifts should rotate forward as much as possible. Shifts that include hours between 23:00 and 07:00, wholly or partly, can only be assigned to employees aged 18 and older. Shifts are performed consecutively, unless the nature of the work associated with the function opposes this. If, in the employer's opinion, such circumstances exist, they will present this with reasons to the employee for discussion before proceeding to determine non-consecutive shifts. Shift handover takes place within working hours."
    ],
    [
      "Breaks (Article 6.8): In addition to the statutory break under the Working Hours Act, the employer provides the employee with the opportunity for additional breaks, to be taken in the morning, afternoon, evening, and/or night. Breaks lasting less than a quarter of an hour are considered working time. If breaks of fifteen minutes or longer are considered working time, uninterrupted rest during these breaks must be guaranteed."
    ],
    [
      "Roster-Free Weekends (Article 6.9): The employee is entitled to at least 22 weekends free from all duties per year. This may be deviated from in consultation with the employee, provided that the employee has at least thirteen Sundays free from all duties per year."
    ],
    [
      "Maximum Working Hours (Article 6.10): The working time per shift, excluding overtime, is a maximum of ten hours, including overtime, twelve hours. The working time per night shift, excluding overtime, is a maximum of nine hours, including overtime, ten hours. The working time per week is a maximum of sixty hours. The employer does not use the possibilities offered by the Atw (Article 5:8 paragraph 3) and the Atb (Articles 4.7:1, 4.7:2, and 5.20:2) to extend the working time per shift, per night shift, or per week. The working time per four weeks is a maximum of an average of 55 hours per week, and per sixteen weeks, a maximum of an average of 48 hours per week. The working time per sixteen weeks is a maximum of an average of forty hours per week if the employee performs sixteen or more night shifts in a period of sixteen weeks. For doctors in this situation, the working time is a maximum of an average of 48 hours per week in a period of thirteen weeks."
    ],
    [
      "Maximum Number of Night Shifts (Article 6.11): The employee may perform a maximum of five consecutive night shifts. At the employee's request, the employer may agree with the employee that the employee performs a maximum of seven consecutive night shifts, but no more than 43 night shifts per sixteen weeks. The employer does not use the possibility offered by the Atw (Article 5:8 paragraph 9) to increase the number of night shifts. For employees who use the transitional provision for permanent night work as referred to in Article 8.1:1 Atb, paragraphs 1 and 2 of this article do not apply."
    ],
    [
      "Shifted Shifts (Article 6.12): The employer may incidentally and in special cases, if business interest makes this necessary, make a change to the already established work and rest time pattern. A shifted shift occurs if a number of consecutive hours, during which the employee would have to perform work according to the work and rest time pattern, are shifted to another time when the employee would be free according to this pattern. If the employer makes a change to an already established work and rest time pattern and a shift occurs within 24 hours after the employee has been informed thereof, the employee receives, in addition to the hourly wage for the hours of that shifted shift, only an overtime surcharge percentage as referred to in Article 9.4 paragraph 2. In case of a change in the work and rest time pattern, the employee receives compensation for expenses already incurred in connection with leisure activities."
    ],
    [
      "Overtime (Article 9.1): Overtime is understood to mean work that is incidentally performed beyond the working hours established in the work and rest time pattern. The excess is measured on a semi-annual basis, calculated from the moment of excess. Compensation for overtime is given if the employee has received an order to work overtime, or could reasonably assume that they would have received an order to work overtime. In the latter case, the employer retrospectively determines the necessity of overtime."
    ],
    [
      "Scope and Exempted Employees for Overtime (Article 9.2): If overtime is performed for a period of half an hour or less preceding or immediately following the working hours established in the work and rest time pattern, this period is not eligible for compensation. If overtime is performed for a period longer than half an hour, this period is rounded up to a full hour. If overtime is performed for a period longer than one hour, this period is rounded up to half or full hours, respectively. Pregnant employees are not assigned overtime after the third month of pregnancy, unless the employee agrees to perform overtime (Article 4:5 Atw)."
    ],
    [
      "Maximum Overtime Hours (Article 9.3): The number of overtime hours may not exceed an average of 10% per week, measured quarterly: a. of 36 hours if the employee's salary does not exceed IP number 48; b. of 42 hours if the employee's salary exceeds IP number 48 but does not exceed IP number 74 of that table; c. of 52 hours if the employee's salary exceeds IP number 74. For employees with a working week deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies. If the percentage of 10% is exceeded, at the request of the employee concerned, either assistance will be provided or a vacancy will be created. If a part-time employee works more than 10% of their contractual working hours in any quarter, at the request of the employee concerned, a contract for the additional hours will be offered. If the person concerned does not request this, assistance will be provided or a vacancy will be created. The works council receives, upon request, an overview of the overtime hours worked per employee per calendar quarter within a department or group to form an opinion on the policy pursued regarding vacancy creation or assistance provision."
    ],
    [
      "Overtime Compensation Scheme for Full-Time Employees (Article 9.4): Compensation for overtime is provided, unless paragraph 3 provides otherwise, in the form of time off, equal to the number of hours the overtime lasted, and additionally in the form of monetary compensation as referred to in paragraph 2. The monetary compensation consists of a percentage of the hourly wage, namely: 25% for overtime worked between 22:00 and 06:00 from Monday to Friday, with the understanding that in a period of seven days, the number of hours thus compensated is a maximum of five; the remaining hours are compensated at 50%; 50% for overtime worked between 18:00 and 22:00 from Monday to Friday, and between 06:00 and 24:00 on Saturday; 75% for overtime worked on Sunday until 24:00 and on free days; 100% for overtime worked on Sunday from 00:00 to 24:00, and on holidays between 00:00 and 24:00, and on December 24 and 31 between 18:00 and 24:00. For the purpose of this article, free days are understood to mean: days, not being a Sunday or holiday, on which the employee would not have to work according to their work and rest time pattern. The right to overtime compensation as mentioned in paragraph 1 is granted if: a. the employee's salary does not exceed IP number 48; b. the employee's salary exceeds IP number 48 but does not exceed IP number 74 of that table: if and insofar as the number of hours worked above the working hours included in the work and rest time pattern amounts to more than six on average per week, measured over the period for which the work and rest time pattern applies; c. the employee's salary is above IP number 74: if and insofar as the number of hours worked above the working hours included in the work and rest time pattern amounts to more than sixteen on average per week, measured over the period for which the work and rest time pattern applies."
    ],
    [
      "Overtime Compensation Scheme for Part-Time Employees (Article 9.5): Overtime compensation consists of the hourly wage applicable to the employee, if and insofar as the number of overtime hours, averaged per week, measured over the period for which the work and rest time pattern applies, does not exceed the difference between the employee's contractual working hours and the full-time working hours. In addition, the employee is entitled to accrual of vacation hours, holiday allowance, and, if applicable, an irregularity allowance for the relevant hourly wage. Compensation under Article 9.4 is granted if and insofar as the number of overtime hours, averaged per week, measured over a period for which the work and rest time pattern applies, exceeds the difference between the employee's contractual working hours and the full-time working hours."
    ],
    [
      "Taking Overtime Compensation (Article 9.6): If, in the employer's opinion, the interests of the work prevent granting the time off referred to in Article 9.4, the time off is converted into a monetary amount, consisting of the proportional part of the salary. The works council receives insight into the application of the preceding paragraph upon request."
    ],
    [
      "Overtime for Doctors in Training (aios) (Article 9.7): When determining whether overtime has occurred, all hours worked by the aios that fall under the definition of 'normative work week' as referred to in Article 6.13 must be included. Hours exceeding the normative work week of an average of 48 hours per week are compensated under Article 9.1. Notwithstanding Article 9.4 paragraph 2, employer and aios may agree to base the compensation on a weighted arithmetic average of the percentages in paragraph 2 for the total number of hours worked after the 48th hour."
    ],
    [
      "Irregular Service (Article 10.1): Irregular service is understood to mean work performed according to the work and rest time schedule during the hours specified in Article 10.4, insofar as they do not exceed 36 hours. For employees with a working week deviating from the full-time norm, the contractually agreed number of hours applies instead of the limit mentioned in paragraph 1. If the employer deems it necessary to assign irregular service to an employee, compensation will be granted in accordance with the provisions of this chapter."
    ],
    [
      "Scope and Exempted Employees for Irregular Service (Article 10.2): Employees classified in function group 65 or lower are entitled to compensation for performing irregular service. Pregnant employees are not assigned irregular service after the third month of pregnancy, unless the employee does not object. Employees aged 57 and older are not assigned night shifts, unless the employee does not object. Employees who waive the right to exemption from night shifts as mentioned in paragraph 3 receive forty hours per year. These hours are deducted from the number of annual working hours. For employees with a working week deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies."
    ],
    [
      "Irregular Service Compensation Scheme (Article 10.3): Compensation for irregular service is provided in the form of monetary compensation or, if the employee requests it, in the form of time off. Time off is determined by dividing the monetary compensation calculated under Article 10.4 by the employee's applicable hourly wage. Unless the interests of the institution prevent it, the request is granted by the employer."
    ],
    [
      "Calculation of Irregular Service Compensation (Article 10.4): The monetary compensation mentioned in Article 10.3 is calculated based on the applicable hourly wage, with the hourly wage derived from IP number 19 serving as the maximum for the calculation, using the following percentages: 22% for irregular service during hours falling between 06:00 and 07:00 and between 20:00 and 22:00 from Monday to Friday; 38% for hours falling between 06:00 and 08:00 and between 12:00 and 22:00 on Saturday; 47% for hours falling between 00:00 and 06:00 and between 22:00 and 24:00 from Monday to Friday; 52% for hours falling between 00:00 and 06:00 and between 22:00 and 24:00 on Saturday; 60% for hours falling between 00:00 and 24:00 on Sundays and holidays and for hours falling between 18:00 and 24:00 on December 24 and 31."
    ],
    [
      "Definitions of On-Call, Availability, and Consignation Duty (Article 11.1): On-call duty (bereikbaarheidsdienst) is understood to mean a continuous period of a maximum of 24 hours during which the employee, in addition to performing the agreed work, is obliged to be reachable to perform the agreed work as soon as possible upon call. Availability duty (aanwezigheidsdienst) is understood to mean a continuous period of a maximum of 24 hours during which the employee, in addition to performing the agreed work, is obliged to be present at the workplace to perform the agreed work as soon as possible upon call. Consignation is understood to mean a period between two consecutive shifts or during a break, during which the employee is solely obliged to be reachable to perform the agreed work as soon as possible upon call in unforeseen circumstances. For doctors in training (aios), Article 6.13 stipulates that hours worked during on-call, availability, and consignation duties count towards the determination of the normative work week."
    ],
    [
      "Scope and Exempted Employees for On-Call, Availability, and Consignation Duty (Article 11.2): For employees working in nursing and care within the meaning of the Atb and for medical practitioners, the norms of the Atb applicable to these employees apply. Article 4.8:2 paragraph 2 (customized arrangement/opt-out) of the Working Hours Decree is not used. During the duties mentioned in Article 11.1, work may only be performed insofar as it cannot be postponed. The employee is free from the duties mentioned in Article 11.1 for at least two weekends per 28 consecutive days. If more than two hours of work have been performed during on-call duty between 00:00 and 06:00, or if a call to come to the institution has been responded to at least twice, the employee is entitled to at least six hours of uninterrupted rest immediately following the last period of actual work performed. The already scheduled hours of the day shift that consequently must be considered rest time are paid by the employer as if they were working hours. Pregnant employees are not assigned on-call, availability, or consignation duty after the third month of pregnancy, unless the employee does not object. Employees aged 58 and older are not assigned on-call, availability, or consignation duty during hours between 00:00 and 06:00, unless the employee does not object."
    ],
    [
      "Working Hours during On-Call, Availability, and Consignation Duty (Article 11.3): If the employee is called during availability duty, a period of at least half an hour is assumed for the calculation of compensation; if this call occurs during on-call duty or consignation duty, a period of at least half an hour is assumed for the aforementioned calculation, to be increased by the actual travel time. If work is performed for a period longer than half an hour, this period is rounded up to a full hour. If work is performed for a period longer than one hour, this period is rounded up to half or full hours, respectively. For doctors in training (aios), it is permitted to determine the number of hours worked during on-call, availability, and consignation duties based on an average calculated from experience data. The number of hours worked during the duties mentioned in Article 11.1 may not exceed an average of 10% per week, measured quarterly: of 36 hours, if the employee's salary does not exceed IP number 48; of 42 hours, if the employee's salary exceeds IP number 48 but does not exceed IP number 74 of that table; of 52 hours, if the employee's salary exceeds IP number 74 (see Explanation Chapter Overtime). If the percentage of 10% is exceeded, at the request of the employee concerned, either assistance will be provided or a vacancy will be created. If a part-time employee works more than 10% of their contractual working hours in any quarter, at the request of the employee concerned, a contract for the additional hours will be offered. If the person concerned does not request this, assistance will be provided or a vacancy will be created. The works council receives, upon request, an overview of the hours worked per employee per calendar quarter under Article 11.1 within a department or group to form an opinion on the policy pursued regarding vacancy creation or assistance provision."
    ],
    [
      "Compensation Scheme for On-Call, Availability, and Consignation Duty (Article 11.4): Employees whose salary does not exceed the amount indicated under IP number 88 receive compensation in time off for hours spent on on-call, consignation, and/or availability duty. The compensation mentioned in the previous paragraph amounts per hour in the case of: a. on-call duty/consignation duty: 1/24 on holidays; 1/48 on Saturdays/Sundays; 1/72 on other days. b. availability duty between 06:00 and 24:00: 2/24 on holidays; 2/48 on Saturdays/Sundays; 1/24 on other days. c. availability duty between 24:00 and 06:00: 3/24 on holidays; 2/24 on Saturdays/Sundays; 2/24 on other days. If the employee performs on-call and/or consignation duty on more than eight weekend days in a period of three consecutive periods of 28 days, in compliance with Article 11.2, they receive, in addition to the compensation mentioned in paragraph 2, a surcharge of 50% of this compensation for the additional on-call and consignation duties on weekend days. Article 11.8 paragraph 3 does not apply to this surcharge."
    ],
    [
      "Compensation Scheme for Work during On-Call, Availability, and Consignation Duty (Article 11.5): Compensation for work performed during the duties mentioned in Article 11.1 is provided, unless paragraph 4 provides otherwise and in compliance with Article 11.4, in the form of time off, equal to the number of hours worked, and additionally in the form of monetary compensation as referred to in paragraph 2. The monetary compensation consists of a percentage of the hourly wage, namely: 25% for work performed between 22:00 and 06:00 from Monday to Friday, with the understanding that in a period of seven days, the number of hours thus compensated is a maximum of five; the remaining hours are compensated at 50%; 50% for work performed between 18:00 and 22:00 from Monday to Friday, and between 06:00 and 24:00 on Saturday; 75% for work performed on Sunday until 24:00 and on free days; 100% for work performed on Sunday from 00:00 to 24:00, and on holidays between 00:00 and 24:00, and on December 24 and 31 between 18:00 and 24:00. For the purpose of this article, free days are understood to mean: days, not being a Sunday or holiday, on which the employee would not have to work according to their work and rest time pattern. The compensation as referred to in paragraph 1 is granted, regardless of the employee's salary, insofar as the number of hours worked does not exceed the average of 36 hours per week, measured over the period for which the work and rest time pattern applies. If the average mentioned in paragraph 3 is exceeded, the right to compensation is granted if: a. the employee's salary does not exceed IP number 48; b. the employee's salary exceeds IP number 48 but does not exceed IP number 74 of that table: if and insofar as the number of hours worked above the working hours included in the work and rest time pattern amounts to more than six on average per week, measured over the period for which the work and rest time pattern applies; c. the employee's salary is above IP number 74: if and insofar as the number of hours worked above the working hours included in the work and rest time pattern amounts to more than sixteen on average per week, measured over the period for which the work and rest time pattern applies. Hours worked by a doctor in training (aios) during the duties mentioned in Article 11.1 that exceed the average 48-hour work week are compensated under paragraphs 1 and 2 of this article."
    ],
    [
      "Taking Compensation for On-Call, Availability, and Consignation Duty (Article 11.8): The time off mentioned in Article 11.4 must be granted and taken within a period of two months after performing the on-call, availability, or consignation duty, unless otherwise agreed between employer and employee. The time off mentioned in Article 11.5 must be granted and taken, after consultation with the employee, no later than in the quarter following the quarter in which the work was performed, unless otherwise agreed between employer and employee. If, in the employer's opinion, the interests of the institution prevent granting time off, the time off as referred to in paragraph 1 is converted into a monetary amount up to a maximum of half, consisting of the proportional part of the salary. The time off as referred to in paragraph 2 can be fully converted into money. The works council receives insight into the application of paragraph 3 upon request. If on-call, availability, or consignation duty is performed on a vacation day designated by the employer as referred to in Article 13.1.4 (from January 1, 2012: Article 13.1.3), the employee's entitlement to that day remains."
    ],
    [
      "Harmonization with Crisis Service (Article 11.10): Employers who have a collaboration with a GGZ institution may, for employees involved in this collaboration, agree on a deviating compensation scheme with the works council for on-call/crisis services, aiming to harmonize the compensation for all persons involved in the service. The deviating compensation scheme may not deviate negatively from the provisions in this chapter. If no agreement is reached, the provisions in this chapter remain applicable."

      "Sunday Work (Appendix C, Page 81): No work is performed on Sunday, unless the contrary has been agreed and follows from the nature of the work, or unless business circumstances make this necessary and the works council agrees, and the employee concerned agrees for that case. In case of work on Sunday, at least 13 free Sundays per 52 weeks (CAO H6, Article 6.9)."
    ],
    [

For V&V (nursing and care), additionally 1 x per period of 7 x 24 hours can be shortened to 10 hours. Weekly uninterrupted rest period: 36 hours per period of 7 x 24 hours, or 72 hours per period of 14 x 24 hours, to be split into periods of at least 32 hours."
