Working time per week: 60 hours (CAO H6, Article 6.10 paragraph 3: this norm cannot be deviated from to the employee's disadvantage). Working time per 4 weeks: average 55 hours per week (CAO H6, Article 6.10 paragraph 5: this norm cannot be deviated from to the employee's disadvantage). Working time per reference period: average 48 hours per week per 16 weeks (CAO H6, Article 6.10 paragraph 5: this norm cannot be deviated from to the employee's disadvantage)."
    ],
    [
      "Additional Rules for Night Shifts (Appendix C, Page 83): A night shift is a shift in which more than 1 hour of work is performed between 00:00 and 06:00. Minimum rest after a night shift ending after 02:00: 14 hours (1 x per 7 x 24 hours can be shortened to 8 hours if the nature of the work or business circumstances require it) (CAO H6, Article 6.10 paragraph 4). Minimum rest after a series of 3 or more consecutive night shifts: 46 hours. Maximum working time per night shift: 10 hours (CAO H6, Article 6.10 paragraphs 2 and 4: 9 hours, including overtime 10 hours; extension to 12 hours is not permitted). Maximum working time per week: 60 hours (CAO H6, Article 6.10 paragraph 3: this norm cannot be deviated from to the employee's disadvantage). Maximum working time per 4 weeks: average 55 hours per week (CAO H6, Article 6.10 paragraph 5: this norm cannot be deviated from to the employee's disadvantage). Maximum working time per reference period: average 40 hours per week per 16 weeks if there are 16 or more night shifts in that period (CAO H6, Article 6.10 paragraph 6: this norm cannot be deviated from to the employee's disadvantage, with the exception of doctors; for doctors: average 48 hours per week per 13 weeks). Maximum number of night shifts: maximum 36 night shifts ending after 02:00 per 16 weeks (CAO H6, Article 6.11: maximum 5 consecutive night shifts or maximum 7 if not more than 43 per 16 weeks; these norms cannot be deviated from to the employee's disadvantage). Maximum number of consecutive shifts in a series that also includes one or more night shifts: 7."
    ],
    [
      "Breaks (Appendix C, Page 85): Working time per shift > 5.5 hours: minimum 0.5 hour (to be split into breaks of minimum 0.25 hour). By collective agreement, the break can be limited to 0.25 hour. If no break is possible: then maximum average 44 hours of work per 16 weeks. Consigned break is a break if the nature of the work makes it necessary (see also H6, Article 6.8). Working time per shift > 10 hours: minimum 0.5 hour (to be split into breaks of minimum 0.25 hour). By collective agreement, the break can be limited to 0.25 hour."
    ],
    [
      "Consignation (Appendix C, Page 85): Period without consignation: per 28 x 24 hours, minimum 14 periods of 24 hours, and twice 48 hours no work. Number of on-call duties: per 7 x 24 hours maximum (a) 3 and (b) 5; per 16 weeks maximum (a) 32 and (b) 32. Consignation before and after a night shift: 11 hours before and 14 hours after a night shift is not permitted. Maximum working time per 24 hours: 13 hours. Maximum working time per week: 60 hours. Maximum working time per reference period (no consignation between 00:00 and 06:00): average 48 hours per week per 16 weeks. Maximum working time per reference period (with consignation between 00:00 and 06:00): average 48 hours per week per 16 weeks. If in 16 weeks there are 16 or more consignations between 00:00 and 06:00: (paragraph 5) maximum average 40 hours per week, or (paragraph 6) maximum average 45 hours per week and after the last call between 00:00 and 06:00, 8 hours rest or in the 18 hours after 06:00, 8 hours rest. Excluded for doctors: average 48 hours per week per 16 weeks (see also H11, Article 11.2 paragraph 5). Minimum working time on call: 0.5 hour."

(The minimum rest of 11 hours before and after an availability duty respectively the minimum rest of 11 hours in the period of 7 x 24 hours can be shortened 1 x per period of 7 x 24 hours to 10 hours and 1 x to 8 hours, with equal extension of the next rest period). Maximum working time per 26 weeks: average 48 hours per week (1248 hours) (Collective agreement H11, Article 11.2 paragraph 2: Article 4.8:2 paragraph 2 Atb (customized arrangement/opt-out) is not used). Maximum working time per 26 weeks for aios: average 48 hours per week (1248 hours) from 1-8-2011."

  "training_information": [
    [
      "Study Agreements: CAO parties agree to conduct research in StAZ context during the term of this CAO on a number of themes. The study agreements are not optional; if parties see reason to do so, the recommendations/conclusions from the research will be implemented. The themes on which the agreed research relates are: 1. 'New Ways of Working': parties agree to investigate what possibilities exist within institutions covered by the CAO Ziekenhuizen to better promote 'new ways of working' (time and place independent) based on an inventory of good practices. Parties thereby stimulate dialogue about promoting possibilities for creating this form of working within institutions. 2. Training and Development: parties agree to investigate how training plans agreed upon within institutions with the works council are implemented, respectively how the resources agreed upon for these training plans are utilized. 3. Wajong: parties agree to investigate how, within institutions covered by the CAO Ziekenhuizen, in collaboration with the UWV Werkbedrijf, opportunities can be created for young people with a work disability (Wajongers) to be considered for vacancies for functions that are realistic given their individual competencies and skills."
    ],
    [
      "Training Plan, Career Advice, and Facilitation (Article 3.2.18): The employer annually establishes the training plan and the necessary budget for it, in consultation with the works council. The training plan includes function-oriented, profession-oriented, and employability-oriented training and a facilities scheme. The employee has the right and obligation to participate in training activities. The employee's request to follow a training course is granted, insofar as it fits within the training plan. The employee's wishes regarding career and training activities and career aspirations are discussed in the annual review (Article 3.2.2). The employee is entitled to individual career advice once every five years. When granting the employee's training activities as referred to in paragraph 3, the employer takes into account the outcomes of the career advice. The facilities scheme as referred to in paragraph 2 includes a study costs and study leave scheme. This scheme at least addresses: studies that are followed at the employer's instruction; the costs that are eligible for reimbursement (for part-time employees, the pro-rata principle is not applied with regard to the (partial) cost reimbursement); the time for which study leave is granted; the repayment of already reimbursed reimbursements. The facilities scheme stipulates that the costs of training followed at the employer's instruction are fully reimbursed. The time for these training courses is considered working time."

    [
      "Working Hours for Students (Article 6.1): For students pursuing a full-time BBL training, no higher working hours may be agreed upon than 1878 hours per year, including lesson time. The training time for a BBL student averages four hours per week (average 208 hours per year), measured over the total training period. Lesson time during the training period is considered working time, with one lesson hour equated to one working hour. For students who do not have the right to a study allowance and are pursuing an HBO-dual training, no higher working hours may be agreed upon than an average of 1670 hours per year (average 32 hours per week). For students who have the right to a study allowance and are pursuing an HBO-dual training, no higher working hours may be agreed upon than a maximum of 1878 hours per year (average 36 hours per week). During a practical year, students with a full-time working week may be deployed for work for a maximum of an average of 1670 hours. The arrangement of working hours is such that the student-employee is enabled to follow the theoretical part of the training."

      "Working Hours for Doctors in Training (aios) (Article 6.13): For doctors in training to become specialists in accordance with the regulations established by the Centraal College Medisch Specialisten (CCMS) and the Medisch Specialisten Registratie Commissie (MSRC), a working week of 1983 hours per year (average 38 hours per week) applies, notwithstanding Article 6.1 paragraph 1. The normative working week of the aios may, in connection with training activities that are integrated into daily work, comprise more than an average of 7.6 hours per day and amount to a maximum of 48 hours per week on average. Hours worked during on-call, availability, and consignation duties count towards the determination of the normative working week. Explanation: The term 'normative working week' includes all training moments directly related to patient care and all organizational activities that are deemed to be in the interest of the institution by the employer. The number of hours worked during on-call, availability, and consignation duties also counts towards the determination of the average 48-hour working week. This also applies to: participation in the management team; participation in team meetings; meetings with house doctors and other disciplines; participation in training for nursing staff, hospital staff, and other employees of the hospital, if no separate remuneration is given by the employer. The employer is primarily responsible for the determination of working hours. The training specialist is primarily responsible for training activities and related training requirements. Between employer, training specialist, and aios, good consultation must be established where training moments are included and where the determination of working hours will be taken into account."

      "Interpretation Committee Regulation (Appendix E): The Interpretation Committee's task is to explain CAO articles against the background of negotiations and the parties' intentions. It consists of eight members and an equal number of alternates, appointed by each party. It rules on matters submitted by parties. A majority of members must be present for rulings. The committee meets as often as both chairmen or four members request, within 14 days. Meeting invitations and agendas are sent at least 7 days in advance (excluding weekends/holidays), which can be shortened in urgent cases. Rulings are made as soon as possible, within 3 months, and experts can be called. Rulings can be published (respecting confidentiality) and are considered for the next CAO."

      "Gratuity and allowances (Article 7.1.8): The employer may grant a gratuity or temporary allowance, for example, based on good performance or the labor market situation. Gratuities and temporary allowances are not structural and are not counted towards the pensionable salary."
    ],
    [
      "Promotion (Article 7.1.9): Upon promotion to a function classified in a higher function group, the new salary is based on the salary scale belonging to the higher function group. The old salary is increased by two increments, after which placement in the higher scale takes place. If granting two increments is not possible due to reaching the scale maximum, the scale is extended with the necessary placement table numbers, after which placement in the higher scale takes place. The new salary may not exceed the maximum of the scale belonging to the higher function. The employer may, in consultation with the works council, make a different arrangement."
    ],
    [
      "Acting allowance (Article 7.1.10): Employees who, at the employer's request, wholly or almost wholly act in the function of an employee classified in a higher function group, receive an allowance for this. If acting takes place for at least half of the daily working hours, the pro-rata principle applies to the allowance. The amount of the acting allowance is equal to the difference between the current salary and the starting salary of the higher classified function, with a minimum of two increments. If granting two increments is not possible due to reaching the scale maximum, the scale is extended with the necessary placement table numbers. The salary, including the allowance, may not exceed the maximum of the scale of the function being acted in. Acting due to vacation or for less than one month is not eligible for compensation. The employer may, in consultation with the works council, make a different arrangement. The acting allowance is not structural and is not counted towards the pensionable salary."
    ],
    [
      "Service year gratuity (Article 7.1.11): Employees who have been continuously employed by the employer are entitled to a one-off gratuity of: a quarter of the salary for an employment of 12.5 years; half of the salary for an employment of 25 years; a full salary for an employment of 40 years. If the contractual working hours have changed in the relevant service years, the salary is increased or decreased proportionally."
    ],
    [
      "Gratuity at end of employment (Article 7.1.12): Upon termination of the employment contract due to reaching the AOW/pensionable age, the employee is entitled to a gratuity equal to half of the salary. Upon termination of the employment contract due to using the bridging benefit (OBU) or Flex-pension from the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn scheme, the employee is entitled to a gratuity equal to half of the salary. Payment takes place if no new employment is entered into. If the employee enters into an employment contract with the same employer in addition to the OBU, the gratuity referred to in lid 2 is granted at the end of the new employment contract. The amount of the gratuity is based on the working hours and the IP number on the day preceding the use of the OBU. If the employee uses the FLEX-pension and remains employed, the gratuity referred to in lid 2 is granted at the end of the employment contract. The amount of the gratuity is based on the working hours and the IP number on the day preceding the use of the FLEX-pension. If the employee uses the exchange-OBU, the gratuity referred to in lid 2 is granted at the moment the employee goes on full OBU and no new employment contract is entered into. The amount of the gratuity is based on the working hours and the IP number on the day preceding the use of the exchange-OBU."
    ],
    [
      "Inservice and BBL training (Article 7.2.1): The provisions in Article 7.2.2 and 7.2.3 apply to inservice and BBL training. Both training programs consist of a vocational preparatory period (BVP) and a subsequent number of practical training years."
    ],
    [
      "Vocational preparatory period (BVP) (Article 7.2.2): During the BVP, a learning agreement is entered into with the student. This period is not part of the first practical training year. During the BVP, the student is entitled to a monthly allowance as stated in Appendix 4 of this chapter. This allowance amounts to 97% of the gross minimum (youth) wage applicable to the student. Tuition/college fees are borne by the employer. Learning materials are provided on loan. The BVP belonging to the inservice training programs cannot last longer than six months."
    ],
    [
      "Practical training years (Article 7.2.3): After completion of the BVP, the student is admitted to the first practical training year. During the practical training years, a learning/employment agreement is entered into with the student-employee. In a class period, a maximum of seven class hours per day may occur on average. During the practical training years, the student receives the salary stated in Appendix 2 of this chapter or, if higher, the minimum (youth) wage, with the exception of the sterilization assistant student and the BBL student level 1 and 2. The sterilization assistant student and the BBL student level 1 and 2 receive the salary stated in Appendix 1 of this chapter, insofar as the minimum (youth) wage is not higher. The student receives an annual periodic increase, calculated from the end of the BVP. Tuition/college fees are borne by the employer."
    ],
    [
      "HBO dual training (Article 7.2.4): An HBO dual training consists of a first year (basic year) and a number of practical training years. If a student does an internship during the basic year, they receive an internship allowance if the requirements of Article 7.2.9 are met. A learning/employment agreement is entered into with the student pursuing the HBO dual training after the first year. During the practical training years, the student receives the salary stated in Appendix 2 of this chapter, insofar as the minimum (youth) wage is not higher. If the second year mainly consists of theory, it is possible that the student receives the first practical training year salary for a period shorter than twelve months. The student receives an annual periodic increase calculated from the date of commencement of employment."
    ],
    [
      "HBO-V dual training (Article 7.2.5): Students pursuing the HBO-V dual training receive salary based on Article 7.2.4 lid 4 during the first two practical training years. During the third practical training year, the student receives the amount belonging to the third practical training year for nine months. For the remaining months, the student receives the amount belonging to the fourth practical training year."
    ],
    [
      "Second care training and follow-up training (Article 7.2.6) (expires January 1, 2013): Notwithstanding Article 7.2.3 and 7.2.4, students who already hold a diploma in healthcare and employees who are pursuing a follow-up education are remunerated according to the provisions below. During the period of the new learning/employment agreement, the student is classified in the first year in the entry scale (seniority 0) of the lowest functional scale applied within the institution for the function for which the relevant training is being followed. During the training, the student-employee receives an annual periodic salary increase. If the student-employee earned more before the training than the salary amount resulting from lid 2, they receive a salary guarantee equal to the last earned salary. This guarantee applies until the periodic salary increase in accordance with lid 3 leads to a higher salary than the guaranteed salary. The employer may, in consultation with the works council, deviate from the provisions in lid 2 and lid 4."
    ],
    [
      "Second care training and follow-up training (Article 7.2.6) (effective January 1, 2013): The employer, in consultation with the works council, establishes a remuneration policy for students who already hold a diploma in healthcare and for students who are pursuing a follow-up education. The starting point here is that the student-employee's income does not decrease. In the absence of an institutional policy, the student retains the IP number and progresses through the scale in which they were classified before the start of the training until the diploma of the training has been obtained."
    ],
    [
      "Aspiring student (Article 7.2.7): During an employment contract preceding a training as referred to in this paragraph, the employee receives the salary belonging to the first year of the relevant training."
    ],
    [
      "Other training (Article 7.2.8): For training programs where the student must perform practical work and the employer is not ultimately responsible, the articles in paragraph 7.1 of this chapter apply to the determination of the salary. For the determination of the salary, the hours actually worked are taken into account. Employer and employee may agree that class hours are wholly or partially considered as working hours."
    ],
    [
      "Internship (Article 7.2.9): Students for whom an internship is a mandatory part of their study are entitled to an internship allowance, provided the internship amounts to at least 144 hours per academic year. The internship allowance is not granted if a BBL student, as an employee, has an employment relationship that entitles them to continued wage payment during the internship period. Students in the sense of this article include MBO and HBO students. The amount of the internship allowance for a full-time internship is €308 per month (January 1, 2011). For a part-time intern, the pro-rata principle applies. The internship allowance is indexed annually with the consumer price index. The internship allowance includes the costs incurred by the intern, with the exception of the allowance referred to in Article 12.1.1 lid 5, as it reads from January 1, 2012. The costs for necessary vaccinations are reimbursed by the government or the employer."
    ],
    [
      "Training for medical specialist, hospital pharmacist, clinical chemist, clinical physicist (Article 7.3.1): Employees undergoing training to become a specialist (aios), hospital pharmacist, clinical chemist, or clinical physicist receive the salary stated in the salary scale in Appendix 3 of this chapter during the training. Basic doctors in training for emergency medicine and sports medicine are remunerated in accordance with this article. When classifying the employee mentioned in lid 1, relevant experience is taken into account. For each relevant year of experience, the first scale amount is increased by one increment. To count as a year of experience, twelve months of relevant experience must have been gained. Experience that fully counts for the classification of the doctor in training: work as a doctor's assistant in a comparable hospital function; work as a basic doctor in patient care in a comparable hospital function; work as a doctor in scientific research; work as a project doctor for a scientific research project in a hospital; work as a basic doctor in a hospital. If, on the date of commencement of employment, the required number of months for a year of experience has not been completed, the periodic date is set such that, with good performance, the next increment is granted when the year of experience is completed. The employee receives an annual periodic increase. If the agreed salary of a doctor prior to training as a medical specialist is higher than the salary amount resulting from lids 1 to 5 of this article, they retain that agreed salary until the periodic salary increase in accordance with lid 6 leads to a higher salary."
    ],
    [
      "Training for nursing home doctor or healthcare psychologist-BIG (Article 7.3.2): For employees undergoing training to become a nursing home doctor or healthcare psychologist, at least the entry scale of the lowest functional scale for doctor or psychologist functions applies. For salary determination, the hours actually worked are taken into account. Employer and employee may agree that class hours are wholly or partially considered as working hours."
    ],
    [
      "End-of-year bonus (Article 7.4.1): The end-of-year bonus for employees who have been employed for a full year on December 31, 2011, is 7.33% of the annual salary. For this purpose, annual salary means twelve times the salary applicable on December 1. The end-of-year bonus is 7.83% in 2012 and 8.33% in 2013. If the employee has been employed for only part of the period over which the end-of-year bonus is calculated and/or has worked part-time during that period or part thereof, they are entitled to a pro-rata end-of-year bonus. If the employee leaves employment before the payment date, the end-of-year bonus is paid pro-rata to the number of months the employment contract lasted after December, based on the monthly salary applicable at the end of employment. Payment of the end-of-year bonus takes place in December, with the exception of the situation mentioned in lid 3."
    ],
    [
      "Holiday allowance (Article 7.5.1): The holiday allowance for employees who have been employed for a full year on May 31 is 8% of the annual salary. For this purpose, annual salary means twelve times the salary applicable on May 1. For employees aged 22 or older, the holiday allowance for a full year of employment is at least €1,699 (as of July 1, 2011), €1,703 (as of October 1, 2011), €1,729 (as of July 1, 2012), or €1,763 (as of July 1, 2013). The minimum amount is increased in May of the payment year by: any general wage adjustments that have taken place within the framework of the Hospital CAO; the effect of any increase in the holiday allowance percentage. If the employee has been employed for only part of the period over which the holiday allowance is calculated and/or has worked part-time during that period or part thereof, they are entitled to a pro-rata holiday allowance. If the employee leaves employment before the payment date, the holiday allowance is paid pro-rata to the number of months the employment contract lasted after May, based on the monthly salary applicable at the end of employment. Payment of the holiday allowance takes place in May, with the exception of the situation mentioned in lid 4. The employer may, in consultation with the works council, make a different arrangement for employees who have indicated they wish to receive their holiday allowance monthly. If paid monthly, the allowance is 8% of the applicable monthly salary."
    ],
    [
      "Life-course scheme surcharge (Article 13.3.3 lid 7): The medical specialist who does not participate in the life-course scheme receives a monthly surcharge on their salary of 0.5% over their applicable salary. No holiday allowance is accrued over this surcharge."

  "pension_information": [
    [
      "Pension Fund Healthcare and Welfare (Article 3.3.2): In principle, all employees working under an employment contract are compulsorily insured for their pension with the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn. The scope, rights, and obligations of the employer and employee regarding pension are regulated in the statutes and pension regulations. The pension premium is recovered for 50% from the employee."
    ],
    [
      "Pension during incapacity for work (Article 4.6): From the second year of illness, the employee can voluntarily continue to accrue pension up to a maximum of the level applicable on the last day of the first year of illness. The employee receives the determined employer's contribution to the pension premium based on the chosen level of continuation."
    ],
    [
      "Premiums during unpaid leave (Article 13.3.2): The premiums due for the period of unpaid leave as referred to in Article 13.3.6, at the expense of the employer, may be recovered from the employee. Under the conditions included in the pension regulations, the employee may voluntarily continue pension insurance during the period of extension of maternity leave, parental leave, and life-course leave. The employee receives, notwithstanding lid 1, the determined employer's contribution to the pension premium based on the chosen level of continuation. Lid 2 and 3 are only applicable if the employee, after the unpaid leave, continues the employment for at least six months. If the employment ends within this period, the part of the premiums for the period of unpaid leave that was at the expense of the employer will be repaid by the employee."
    ],
    [
      "Life-course scheme (Article 13.3.3): The employee has the right to participate in the life-course scheme. A request to use the life-course balance for full early retirement will be honored by the employer. Under the conditions included in the pension regulations, the employee may voluntarily continue pension provision during the period of life-course leave. The employee receives the employer's contribution to the pension premium applicable at that time, based on the chosen level, provided that after the life-course leave, the employment is continued for at least six months. If the employment ends within this six-month period, the part of the pension premium for the period of life-course leave that was at the expense of the employer will be repaid by the employee. The employee who participates in the life-course scheme receives a monthly employer's contribution of 0.5% of their applicable salary from June 1, 2006."
    ],
    [
      "Unemployment benefit and pension (Article 14.5): During the unemployment benefit period, if and as long as the unemployment benefit recipient wishes to continue participation in Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn, pension entitlements remain related to the salary, being the contribution basis according to the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn Regulations. The employer is responsible for the payment of the premium due. The employee's share of the unemployment benefit recipient is related to the amount of the unemployment benefit, reduced by the allowances included in the unemployment benefit that are not included in the contribution basis. If and as long as the Pension Accrual during Unemployment Scheme (FVP) applies to the unemployment benefit recipient, the provision in lid 1 regarding the premium due does not apply to the unemployment benefit recipient. The provision in lid 1 does not apply if the unemployment benefit recipient who is younger than fifty years can participate in the pension scheme attached to their possible new employment. In the event that the employer's supplement is not sufficient to cover the premium due by the unemployment benefit recipient, the person concerned must pay the missing part from the statutory benefit. The premium due on income above the contribution limit mentioned in the Pension Accrual during Unemployment Scheme is, after deduction of the proportional employee's share, borne by the employer."
    ]
  ],
  "leave_information": [
    [
      "Above-statutory vacation hours (22 hours) abolished from January 1, 2012, and added to the Personal Life-Phase Budget (PLB). Statutory vacation hours follow the Civil Code (BW)."
    ],
    [
      "Personal Life-Phase Budget (PLB): Introduced January 1, 2010, for all employees under the Hospital CAO. This PLB is used for spending goals in time. The purpose of the scheme is to give employees the opportunity to use a saved budget when their life phase gives cause. Social partners aim to promote the sustainable employability of employees. In 2012, a maximum of 22 hours of the PLB budget can be used annually, and for the first time in December, at the employee's request, in the multi-choice system of employment conditions (MKSA). Part-time employees who work extra hours generally receive PLB time for the extra hours in the form of a surcharge on their hourly wage. During incapacity for work/illness, employees accrue their PLB budget for a maximum period of six months. 'A period of maximum six months' refers to the first six months after reporting sick. After that, no further accrual takes place for the hours of incapacity. For the purpose of determining the six months, periods are aggregated if they follow each other with an interruption of less than one month. To transfer PLB hours to another institution applying the Hospital CAO, the employee must have at least 50 PLB hours, or the transfer must relate to at least 50 PLB hours. For employees who do not have 50 PLB hours, the employer, upon receipt of the employee's resignation, informs the employee of the consequences of the resignation for the PLB budget. As of January 1, 2011, accrued PLB leave can no longer be fully taken prior to the pensionable age. The annual working hours may be reduced by a maximum of 50% compared to the annual working hours in the preceding calendar year. This condition does not apply to employees who, due to compelling business reasons, could not use PLB hours during their employment."
    ],
    [
      "Pregnancy and maternity leave: Article 13.4.2 lid 1 is amended as follows: 'The employee is entitled to a benefit from the UWV during the period of pregnancy and maternity leave. The employer supplements the benefit to the employee's salary determined by time.'"
    ],
    [
      "Calamity leave: Article 13.4.5 lid 2 is amended as follows: 'In case of death of blood relatives and relatives by marriage in the direct line - a brother or sister of the employee and/or of his spouse or partner, the employee is entitled to paid leave on the day of death and on the day of the funeral or cremation, insofar as the employee was scheduled to work on this day/these days. In case of death of the spouse or partner - a (foster) child - a (foster) parent of the employee and/or of his spouse or partner, the employee is entitled to paid leave from the day of death up to and including the day of the funeral or cremation, insofar as the employee was scheduled to work on this day/these days.'"
    ],
    [
      "Public holidays (Article 1.1.1.o): New Year's Day, first and second Easter day, Ascension Day, first and second Pentecost day, first and second Christmas day, and national public holidays recognized as such by the government, including King's Day and every year Liberation Day, as well as special public holidays determined by the employer in consultation with the works council (such as the institution's X-year jubilee)."
    ],
    [
      "Off-duty periods (Article 1.1.1.p): 'Off duty' means free from duties, with the exception of standby and on-call duty. 'Free from all duties' means free from duties, including special duties as referred to in Chapter 11. A half day off: a period of eighteen hours free from duty. A day off: a period of 32 hours free from duty. One and a half consecutive days off: a period of 46 hours free from duty. Two consecutive days off: a period of 56 hours free from duty. Two and a half consecutive days off: a period of 66 hours free from duty. Three consecutive days off: a period of 72 hours free from duty. A free weekend: a period of 56 hours free from duty, falling on Saturday and Sunday. Formula for the number of consecutive days off not mentioned above: a period free from duty where the number of hours is the number of half days off multiplied by twelve hours plus six hours."
    ],
    [
      "Wage payment during illness/incapacity (Article 4.2): Employees who are unable to work due to incapacity and have immediately informed the employer are entitled to 70% of their time-based salary for a maximum of 104 weeks, according to the Civil Code. Time-based salary includes the employee's salary plus structural wage components such as irregular service allowance and on-call duty allowance, measured monthly over six months prior to the incapacity. From January 1, 2005, the wage is supplemented to 100% for the first 52 weeks of incapacity. For the subsequent 52 weeks, employees receive 70% of their time-based salary, but at least the statutory minimum wage. For hours worked according to a reintegration plan, suitable work, or work without wage value (e.g., therapeutic work, training, internship), employees receive 100% of their time-based salary. The wage is adjusted for general wage increases and contract changes (if the period between the first day of incapacity and the contract change is less than three months). Periodic increases are granted after 52 weeks for hours worked. The wage is reduced by any statutory or equivalent benefits. The employer may grant a wage supplement for occupational, chronic, or life-threatening illnesses."
    ],
    [
      "Reduction or cessation of wage payment (Article 4.4): The employer may wholly or partially reduce or cease the right to supplementation as referred to in Article 4.2 lid 2 and lid 3 if: the employee does not comply with the obligations in Article 4.5; the employee wholly or partially loses the right to a benefit under the WAO, WIA, WW, or ZW, unless this is attributable to the employer."
    ],
    [
      "Reintegration (Article 4.5): To achieve sustainable reintegration into their own or a suitable function, the company doctor, based on UWV criteria, indicates which activities the employee can perform. The employer, after advice from the company doctor and consultation with the employee, determines the precise nature of the work to be performed. The employer is obliged to offer suitable work for the number of hours the employee is able to work. If there are no possibilities within the institution, the employer endeavors to realize suitable work outside the institution. Reintegration activities continue after two years if the employee has sufficient residual capacity and actively participates in the reintegration process. To achieve optimal reintegration, the employee, in consultation with the employer, can use all existing reintegration instruments such as access to mobility centers, and training or internship opportunities, within the institution. Associated costs are borne by the employer. The employee is obliged to cooperate with the offered reintegration activities and to accept a suitable function. They can also take their own initiatives and submit them to the employer. The employee has priority for internal vacancies."
    ],
    [
      "Paid leave for employee organization membership (Article 5.3): The employer enables the employee to participate in activities of the employee organization of which they are a member. The employee receives paid leave totaling 228 hours per year if these activities take place during hours when they are scheduled to work according to their employment contract. For part-time employees, the pro-rata principle is not applied. Employee organizations include: a union that is a party to this CAO; a central union of employees that is a party to this CAO; professional associations affiliated with a union of employees that is a party to this CAO. This explicitly excludes professional associations mentioned in the preamble of this CAO under II the following employee organizations: the Federation of Social Work and Welfare, the Union for Psychodiagnostic Workers. Activities include: statutory meetings, as well as meetings of statutory regional organizations, insofar as the employee is designated as a board member and/or delegate; conferences, national and regional meetings, and working groups, insofar as the employee has been invited by the main board; courses, insofar as the employee participates in them at the request of the main board. Insofar as it concerns activities of (professional) associations as referred to under lid 3, only activities related to collective employment conditions are included under the above-mentioned activities. For employees who are union consultants in an institution with 1500 employees or more, an exemption of eight hours per week applies. For union consultants in an institution with at least five hundred but fewer than 1500 employees, an exemption of four hours per week applies. A maximum of two union consultants (one per employee organization) can be exempted per institution."
    ],
    [
      "Paid leave for professional association board members (Article 5.4): The employer enables employees who are board members of a professional association to participate in activities of the professional organization of which they are a member. Professional organizations include the professional associations mentioned in the preamble of this CAO under II the following employee organizations. Activities include meetings and work performed in special committees related to professional content. The employee receives paid leave totaling 36 hours per year if these activities take place during hours when they are scheduled to work according to their employment contract. For employees with working hours deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle is not applied."
    ],
    [
      "Annual hours system and vacation (Article 6.3): If vacation hours are taken on a day when a certain number of hours would have been worked according to the work and rest time pattern, this number of hours is registered as vacation."
    ],
    [
      "Annual hours system and public holidays (Article 6.4): The number of hours to be worked annually is reduced by 7.2 hours for each public holiday not falling on a Saturday or Sunday. For employees with working hours deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies with regard to deployability on public holidays and with regard to the provisions in lid 1. For employees who commence employment during the calendar year, the reduction applies based on the public holidays occurring in the remaining part of the calendar year. If desired, employees can exchange public holidays for the public holidays applicable to their religious beliefs. The employer may, in consultation with the works council, make a different arrangement. This arrangement replaces the provisions in this article. If no agreement has been reached, the provisions in this article remain applicable."
    ],
    [
      "Annual hours system and illness/incapacity (Article 6.5): If the employee does not perform their work due to illness/incapacity, the number of registered sick hours is based on the agreed work pattern. If no work pattern has been agreed, the average contractual working hours apply. If the employee does not perform their work due to pregnancy and maternity leave, the average contractual working hours apply."
    ],
    [
      "Savings leave (Article 8.5): Based on Article 7:634 BW, employees with a full-time employment are entitled to at least 144 vacation hours per year (statutory vacation hours). For employees with working hours deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies (see Article 13.1.1 lid 3). Agreements on savings leave can only be made for hours exceeding this statutory minimum (above-statutory vacation hours). Notwithstanding Article 13.1.5 lid 1, employer and employee may agree that above-statutory vacation hours can be saved (from January 1, 2012, only applicable to above-statutory vacation hours as referred to in Article 13.1.2 lid 2). The employee has the right to take the saved hours in a continuous period. If an agreement has been made about the timing of the leave period, this can only be deviated from by mutual consent. If the agreed period changes at the employer's request, the damage suffered by the employee as a result of the change in the leave period will be reimbursed by the employer. If the employee takes the saved hours and/or leave under lid 3 for career reorientation, these hours are increased by 20%. Career reorientation means undertaking activities (e.g., training, courses, internship, study trip) determined in consultation with the employer. Career reorientation does not include preparation for the OBU, or any other provision in the context of pre-retirement. The employee is given the opportunity to extend the leave saved in the agreed period with unpaid leave up to a maximum of six months."
    ],
    [
      "Vacation (old, expires January 1, 2012) (Article 13.1.1): For the purpose of determining working hours in a work schedule, the actual number of vacation hours taken by the employee is counted. If vacation is taken on a day when a daily working time of nine hours would have been scheduled, then nine vacation hours are counted towards the determination of working hours and similarly for the settlement of vacation hours taken. The result of the calculation of the number of vacation hours applicable to the employee in any year is rounded up to whole hours. For employees with working hours deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies."
    ],
    [
      "Vacation hours (old, expires January 1, 2012) (Article 13.1.2): The employee is entitled to 166 vacation hours per calendar year with retention of salary. Of these, 144 hours are statutory (Article 7:634 BW) and 22 hours are above-statutory. Employees who, as of January 1, 2004, use Article 6.13 or 6.14 (55-plus scheme) of the CAO Hospitals 2008-2009 are entitled to 184 vacation hours per calendar year instead of 166 hours."
    ],
    [
      "Accrual of vacation hours (old, expires January 1, 2012) (Article 13.1.3): For each calendar month in which the employee is or will be employed, vacation amounts to 1/12 of the annually determined vacation. A month in which employment commenced before the 16th or ended after the 15th is considered a full calendar month for vacation determination. If employment commenced after the 15th of the month or ended before the 16th of the month, vacation rights accrue pro-rata. For employees who have not performed their work due to incapacity for work, the accrual of their vacation rights continues for the last six months of that incapacity (Article 7:635 lid 4 BW). The provision in lid 2 also applies, in addition to statutory provisions, to partially incapacitated employees (Article 7:635 BW). The right to accrue vacation rights during pregnancy and maternity leave is granted independently of any preceding or subsequent incapacity for work (Article 7:635 lid 2 BW)."
    ],
    [
      "Designation of vacation days (old, expires January 1, 2012) (Article 13.1.4): The employer may determine that the employee takes vacation leave on two working days to be designated by the employer. This leave is included in the number of hours mentioned in Article 13.1.2. This designation takes place: in consultation with the works council; no later than the end of January; for one or more groups of employees."
    ],
    [
      "Taking vacation hours (old, expires January 1, 2012) (Article 13.1.5): Vacation must be taken in the calendar year in which the entitlement arose. Vacation not taken in the interest of the institution is granted in the following calendar year. This may be deviated from in very exceptional cases. For taking vacation hours during partial incapacity for work, the number of hours to which the employee is entitled during their partial incapacity can only be taken for the part for which they are working. Vacation is granted, unless the interests of the department or service where the employee is employed oppose it, in accordance with the employee's wishes - if desired, uninterrupted. The employee can claim at least three consecutive weeks of vacation, including the weekends preceding and following it. The employer may make an arrangement that allows a maximum of three sick days to be designated as vacation leave if the employee takes vacation during the incapacity period with the consent of the company doctor, provided that the employee retains at least the number of vacation days mentioned in Article 7:634 BW. The general arrangement and the distribution of vacation within the institution require the consent of the works council."
    ],
    [
      "Cashing out vacation hours (old, expires January 1, 2012) (Article 13.1.6): Pursuant to Article 7:640 lid 2 BW, the employee is allowed to cash out accrued vacation, provided that the employee retains at least 144 hours of vacation (Article 7:634 BW)."
    ],
    [
      "Change of vacation period (old, expires January 1, 2012) (Article 13.1.7): The employer may, if circumstances arise which could not have been foreseen at the time of determining the vacation period and as a result of which the functioning of the institution or of the service or department is seriously jeopardized, change the vacation period determined by him. The employer determines the new vacation period in consultation with the employee. The damage suffered by the employee as a result of this change will be reimbursed by the employer."
    ],
    [
      "Incapacity for work during vacation (old, expires January 1, 2012) (Article 13.1.8): If the employee reports sick during their vacation, in accordance with the sick reporting regulations mentioned in Article 4.8, the sick days from the moment of reporting sick are not considered vacation days."
    ],
    [
      "Vacation (new, effective January 1, 2012) (Article 13.1.1): Insofar as this paragraph does not provide otherwise or additionally, the provisions of the Civil Code regarding vacation apply (Article 7:634 to 7:645 BW). These statutory provisions may not be deviated from. For the purpose of determining working hours, the actual number of vacation hours taken by the employee is counted. If vacation is taken on a day when a daily working time of nine hours would have been scheduled according to the work and rest time pattern, then nine vacation hours are counted towards the determination of working hours and similarly for the settlement of vacation hours taken. For employees with working hours deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies."
    ],
    [
      "Scope and accrual of vacation hours (new, effective January 1, 2012) (Article 13.1.2): The employee is entitled to 144 vacation hours per calendar year with retention of salary. Employees who, as of January 1, 2004, use Article 6.13 or 6.14 (55-plus scheme) of the CAO Hospitals 2008-2009 are entitled to 184 vacation hours per calendar year instead of 144 hours. A month in which employment commenced before the 16th or ended after the 15th is considered a full calendar month for vacation determination. If employment commenced after the 15th of the calendar month or ended before the 16th of the month, vacation rights accrue pro-rata."
    ],
    [
      "Designation of vacation days (new, effective January 1, 2012) (Article 13.1.3): The employer may determine that the employee takes vacation leave on a maximum of two working days to be designated by the employer. This leave is included in the number of hours mentioned in Article 13.1.2. The employer of a rehabilitation institution may establish a collective closing arrangement. During this period, employees are obliged to take vacation days. Application of this article takes place in consultation with the works council. The decision applies to one or more groups of employees and is made no later than the end of January."
    ],
    [
      "Taking vacation hours (new, effective January 1, 2012) (Article 13.1.4): The employer is obliged to enable the employee to take the granted vacation hours each year. The employee can claim at least three consecutive weeks of vacation, including the weekends preceding and following it. The general arrangement and the distribution of vacation within the institution require the consent of the works council."

      "Personal Life-Phase Budget (PLB) (Article 13.2.1): As of January 1, 2011, the employee receives a PLB of thirty hours for that calendar year. From January 1, 2012, the employee annually receives a PLB of 57 hours. For commencement and termination of employment within a calendar year, the PLB is granted pro-rata to the number of months of employment. For employees with working hours deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies."
    ],
    [
      "Principles of Personal Life-Phase Budget (PLB) (Article 13.2.2): The employer administers the PLB. Upon change of employment percentage and at the end of employment, the employee receives a new overview of their accrued budget. The PLB is primarily used for spending goals in time. The purpose of the scheme is to give the employee the opportunity to use a saved budget when their life phase gives cause. The introduction of PLB hours aims to promote the sustainable employability of the employee. Taking leave can be done at the employee's discretion and wishes, subject to the following provisions: employees who wish to take substantial leave must submit a written request to the employer at least four months before the start of the leave, stating the duration and scope of the leave; the four-month period does not apply if there is no substantial leave; the employer will grant the request for leave unless such a compelling business or service interest opposes taking leave that the employee's interest must yield to it according to standards of reasonableness and fairness. Employees may take PLB leave prior to the pensionable age for a maximum of half of the working hours of the preceding calendar year, unless the employee could not take the remaining leave earlier due to compelling business reasons. Part-time employees who work extra hours generally receive PLB for the extra worked hours in the form of a surcharge on their hourly wage. The PLB has no expiry date. Annually, and for the first time in December 2012, a maximum of 22 hours of the PLB can be used in the Multi-Choice System of Employment Conditions (MKSA) at the employee's request. For this use, the pro-rata principle does not apply. Upon termination of employment, death, and through use in the MKSA, there may be a payout of accrued rights. The total value of the PLB can be (partially) deposited into the life-course scheme upon termination of employment. The value of an accrued hour is the employee's hourly wage at the time of buy-out. Upon termination of employment, the PLB is transferred to a new hospital employer at the employee's request. The transfer must relate to at least fifty PLB hours. If the employee does not have at least fifty PLB hours, the employer immediately informs them of the consequences of this provision upon receipt of the resignation. The accrual of the PLB is continued for a maximum of six months in case of illness/incapacity for work. For the purpose of determining the six months, periods are aggregated if they follow each other with an interruption of less than four weeks. Once accrued leave does not expire during illness/incapacity for work."

      "Leave definitions (Article 13.3.1): Paid leave means the number of hours to be taken by the employee in a work schedule under this regulation, during which no work needs to be performed. These hours count towards the total working hours. Unpaid leave means the right to be free from all duties. Unpaid leave granted under this regulation is disregarded when determining the total working hours. Children residing in the employee's household for whom an adoption application has been submitted are considered the employee's children for the purpose of this paragraph. For employees who are members of a religious community, this paragraph applies mutatis mutandis. In that case, a priest's jubilee is equated with a wedding celebration. For employees with working hours deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies."

If necessary, the employer grants paid leave for this. Events include: relocation of the employee at the employer's request: two days; marriage or registration of partnership of the employee: one day; marriage or registration of partnership of blood relatives in the first and second degree of the employee, of the spouse or partner: one day; 12.5 and 25 years of marriage of the employee: one day; 12.5, 25, 40, 50, 60 years of marriage of parents or foster parents of the employee, of the spouse or partner: one day; 12.5 and 25 years of service jubilee of the employee: one day; board and ministerial committees on the field of healthcare and medical disciplinary committees: one day. For the events mentioned in lid 1, the employee must notify the employer fourteen days before the event that they wish to attend, and the pro-rata principle is not applied. If the employee marries or enters into another cohabitation arrangement, by notarial deed or municipal or ecclesiastical registration, 14.4 hours of paid leave are granted. (The employer only needs to grant the leave hours once as long as it concerns the same cohabitation arrangement). In cases where the employee, due to a visit to a (dentist) doctor, could not reasonably make an appointment outside the fixed working hours, the employer grants the employee paid leave."

      "Vacation (Article 7:634 BW): The employee accrues vacation for each year in which they were entitled to salary for the full agreed working hours, amounting to at least four times the agreed weekly working hours or, if the agreed working hours are expressed in hours per year, at least a corresponding period. Employees who were entitled to salary for part of a year accrue vacation for that part, amounting to a proportional part of what they would have been entitled to if they had been entitled to salary for the full agreed working hours throughout the year. By collective labor agreement or by regulation by or on behalf of a competent administrative body, for employees whose employment contract ends after having lasted at least one month, lid 2 may be deviated from such that the vacation entitlement is calculated over periods of one month."

      "Accrual of vacation hours (Article 7:635 BW): Notwithstanding Article 634, the employee accrues vacation for the period during which they were not entitled to a fixed monetary salary because: a. they were called up for military service or substitute service, other than for exercise and training; b. they take vacation as referred to in Article 641 lid 3; c. they participate, with the employer's consent, in a meeting organized by a trade union of which they are a member; d. they are, other than due to incapacity for work as referred to in lids 2 to 4, involuntarily unable to perform the agreed work; e. they take leave as referred to in Article 643; f. they take leave as referred to in Chapter 5, Section 2, of the Work and Care Act. Notwithstanding Article 634, employees who do not accrue salary for a full year due to adoption leave or leave for taking in a foster child accrue vacation for the full agreed working hours for the period during which they are entitled to a benefit as referred to in Chapter 3, Section 2, of the Work and Care Act. Notwithstanding Article 634, employees who do not perform the agreed work due to illness, regardless of whether they are entitled to salary, accrue vacation for the period of the last six months in which the work was not performed, provided that periods are aggregated if they follow each other with an interruption of less than one month. Young employees accrue vacation for the time they spend attending the education which the employer is legally obliged to enable them to attend.       "Suspension (Article 3.1.4): The employer may suspend the employee for a maximum of one week with retention of salary, for such serious reasons that continuation of the work by the employee is no longer deemed responsible by the employer. The employer may extend the suspension once by a maximum of one week. The employer cannot impose an extension simultaneously with the pronouncement of the first suspension. The decision to suspend or the decision to extend it is communicated to the employee without delay and with reasons, and confirmed by registered letter. The employee is given the opportunity to account for themselves to the employer within four days after the date of the registered letter (excluding Saturdays, Sundays, and public holidays). They may be assisted by a legal advisor. The suspension may be extended until the effective date of the termination of the employment contract when: the employer has immediately initiated a dismissal procedure with the UWV Werk en Inkomen; or a request for dissolution of the employment contract has been submitted to the subdistrict court or the Scheidsgerecht Gezondheidszorg. During the suspension, the employee retains their salary. The employer is authorized to deny the employee access to the buildings and grounds of the institution during the suspension period, insofar as this does not relate to the employee's living space. If it appears that the employee was clearly wrongly suspended by the employer, the employer will, at the employee's request, publicly rehabilitate them and compensate them for demonstrable damage."

      "Non-active status (Article 3.1.5): The employer may place the employee on non-active status for a maximum of three weeks if, in the employer's opinion, the progress of the work - for whatever reason - is seriously impeded. The employer may extend the non-active status once by a maximum of three weeks. With the consent of the employee or their representative, another extension of a maximum of three weeks may be agreed upon. The decision to place on non-active status or the decision to extend it is communicated by the employer to the employee with reasons and confirmed in writing as soon as possible. The employer may place the employee on non-active status for the remaining duration of the notice period after a legally valid notice period has commenced. The condition is that, in the employer's opinion, compelling interests make this necessary. During the non-active status, the employee retains their salary and all other rights arising from the employment contract/CAO. The employer must promote the employee's return to work during the non-active status period. After the non-active status period expires, the employee is entitled to resume their work. Non-active status cannot be used as a disciplinary measure."

      "Termination (Article 3.1.7): The employment contract is terminated in writing. A fixed-term employment contract can only be terminated prematurely if that right has been agreed upon in writing for each party. An employment contract may not be terminated, with the exception of termination for urgent reasons as referred to in Articles 7:678 BW and 7:679 BW, without the employer being in possession of a dismissal permit. The party terminating the employment contract provides, upon request, also during an agreed probationary period, written reasons for termination. The effective date of dismissal is the first of the calendar month. The employer cannot terminate during the period that the employee is unable to perform their work due to illness, unless the incapacity has lasted two years, or the incapacity arose after a request for a dismissal permit was received by the CWI. The two-year period does not apply in the situations described in Article 7:670b lid 3 BW. The employer conducts an exit interview with the employee who wishes to terminate the employment contract by giving notice. If the statutory employee's notice period is extended up to three months, the employer's notice period may be equated with the employee's notice period. If the employee's notice period is extended to four, five, or six months, the employer's notice period is doubled. If the employer terminates the employment contract, the statutory notice period applies if it leads to a longer period than the notice period included in the employment contract. For employees who were employed by the employer on January 1, 1999, and were 45 years or older at that time, and for whom a longer notice period applied than according to lid 8 of this article, the old period remains applicable."

      "WIA (Article 4.7): If the employer, according to the competent authority, has failed in its reintegration obligation and the employee therefore has no right to a benefit under WAO/WIA, the employment relationship is in principle maintained, and the incapacity for work itself will not be a reason for dismissal. In that case, the employer pays 70% of the time-based salary. This supplement aims to prevent the employee from having to rely on the Work and Social Assistance Act for their livelihood. Income as referred to in the first sentence means the total of wage, WGA follow-up benefit, incapacity for work pension, any other benefits, and a supplement under the Supplements Act.       "Unemployment benefit (Article 14.1): For the purpose of the further provisions of this chapter, 'unemployment benefit' means the sum of, on the one hand, a WW benefit, any ZW benefit, a WIA benefit, a benefit under a wage supplement scheme, an OBU benefit from Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn; income from work or business, insofar as Article 14.2 lid 4 or Article 14.3 applies, and on the other hand, the employer's supplement thereto. 'Salary' means the net income derived from the sum of the gross monthly salary and the other components that are considered wages in the Daily Wage Rules for employee insurance schemes. The income from work or business, insofar as Article 14.2 lid 4 or Article 14.3 applies, means the following incomes: the salary from a new employment relationship that is accepted before the date of dismissal; income from a service relationship or business that arises after the date of dismissal and that, based on the reduction determination of Article 14.7, leads to a reduction of the statutory benefit and the employer's supplement."

    [
      "Scope of unemployment benefit (Article 14.2): Employees whose employment contract does not end at their own request are granted unemployment benefit from the day following the day on which the employment ends if this termination occurs due to: full or partial abolition of their function or position; reorganization whereby work has become wholly or partially redundant; merger, liquidation, full or partial closure of the institution; unsuitability of the employee, which is not attributable to their fault or actions. The employee must have reached the age of fifty and have been employed by the employer or its legal predecessor for fifteen years or longer. The unemployment benefit is granted if the employee has been granted a WW benefit and otherwise does everything necessary to obtain the benefits mentioned in Article 14.1. If the unemployment benefit recipient receives a ZW or WIA benefit on the day the unemployment benefit would commence, this is supplemented to the level and for the duration of the unemployment benefit. If no WW benefit is granted within two years after the aforementioned day, the unemployment benefit ends no later than after two years. Employees who are informed in writing by the employer that the employment contract will be terminated on one of the grounds mentioned in lid 1, and who accept another employment relationship before the day of dismissal with a lower salary, are granted a supplement to that salary, from the day of commencement of employment, up to the level and for the duration of the unemployment benefit."

      "Duration of unemployment benefit (Article 14.3): The unemployment benefit is granted for three months, increased by three months for each full year of service, provided that a maximum of twenty years of service are taken into account when applying this provision. For employees who will reach the pensionable age within five years after the date of dismissal and who, in addition, will have completed at least ten years of service with the institution on the date of dismissal, the duration of the unemployment benefit is extended until that age is reached. For employees who will be eligible for the full bridging benefit (OBU) as referred to in the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn Regulations within five years after the date of dismissal, the duration of the unemployment benefit is extended until this time, subject to the following conditions: on the date of dismissal, at least ten years of service must have been completed with the institution; during the aforementioned period of a maximum of five years, participation in the bridging benefit scheme as referred to in the regulations of the aforementioned Pension Fund must have been voluntarily continued. During the extended unemployment benefit period mentioned in lids 2 and 3, the provisions of Article 14.4 lid 1 apply. For the purpose of determining the number of years of service as referred to in lid 1, all full consecutive months spent in the service of institutions affiliated with the NVZ, NZf, or their legal predecessors count. Years of service spent in the aforementioned institutions, which are not continuously consecutive and during which interruptions the employee received unemployment benefit under this CAO, are deemed to be consecutive for the purpose of this provision. If the entitlement to unemployment benefit has ended because the employee receives income from an employment relationship and this employment relationship does not end at their own request for a reason mentioned in Article 14.2 lid 1, the entitlement to unemployment benefit revives. The duration of the employment relationship is deducted from the total duration of the unemployment benefit entitlement. The revival of unemployment benefit does not apply if and as long as there is an entitlement to unemployment benefit with the new employer."

    [
      "Amount of unemployment benefit (Article 14.4): The unemployment benefit is equal to the last earned salary for the first six months, and subsequently 80% for the next three months, 75% for the following two years, and then 70% of the salary. During the unemployment benefit period, the unemployment benefit is never less than 80% of the minimum wage as referred to in the Minimum Wage and Minimum Holiday Allowance Act. If the unemployment benefit recipient receives a bridging benefit as referred to in the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn Regulations, this benefit is supplemented to the level of the unemployment benefit as described in this article. General wage adjustments as applicable for this CAO, which the unemployment benefit recipient would have received if they had remained employed, will be taken into account when calculating the unemployment benefit. In that case, the applicable premium rates at that time will be taken into account for the calculation of net income. If, as a result of the gradual or non-gradual reduction of individual work, the application of the provisions in the preceding lids would lead to unfavorable results for the unemployment benefit recipient, those provisions will be deviated from in their favor."
    ],

      "Obligations of the employee/unemployment benefit recipient (Article 14.6): The employee is obliged, after notification of dismissal, to immediately register as a job seeker with the Labor Office. The employee/unemployment benefit recipient is obliged to make use of any opportunity offered to obtain income from work or business, unless they demonstrate that this obligation cannot reasonably be demanded of them. The employee/unemployment benefit recipient is obliged to immediately report to the employer the amount of income from work or business and the amount of benefit received under a statutory scheme. If requested, they must provide all desired information and supporting documents. The employee/unemployment benefit recipient who is eligible for the Pension Accrual during Unemployment Scheme is obliged to cooperate with the continuation of pension accrual, as stipulated in this scheme."

If a penalty reduction is applied to an employee's WW benefit, the employer's obligation to supplement as referred to in Article 14.4 will be adjusted accordingly, and the employee cannot invoke wage payment under Article 7:629 BW. If the income from business or work as referred to in lid 1 leads to a (partial) reduction of the statutory benefit, the part of the income that is deducted from the statutory benefit will become part of the unemployment benefit."

      "Cessation of unemployment benefit (Article 14.8): The unemployment benefit ceases: if and when the employee dies; if and when the employee becomes entitled to an old-age pension or disability pension under the Pensioenfonds Zorg en Welzijn Regulations, by virtue of the employment from which they were dismissed with unemployment benefit; if the unemployment benefit recipient fails to comply with the obligations imposed on them in Article 14.6; if one of the benefits mentioned in Article 14.1 is stopped because the unemployment benefit recipient does not do everything necessary to obtain one of these benefits. Pending an appeal procedure under the said statutory regulations, the payment of the unemployment benefit is suspended. The unemployment benefit may be declared forfeited by the employer if: the unemployment benefit recipient fails to cooperate with a medical examination aimed at applying for a disability pension or a statutory benefit due to incapacity for work; the unemployment benefit recipient is found to have established themselves permanently abroad. This provision does not apply to persons who have reached the age of 55."

The social plan addresses, among other things: redeployment, possibly in combination with temporary redeployment or detachment; the provision of natural attrition due to redeployment and natural turnover; outplacement and training, both aimed at redeployment and at increasing opportunities in the labor market; outplacement and other forms of support to increase opportunities in the labor market; financial support in cases where involuntary dismissal is unavoidable; a salary guarantee scheme; a hardship clause in cases where the social plan leads to an unreasonable or unfair situation for the individual employee; the period over which these measures extend; the position of the on-call worker; the position of potential future employees."

Lids 1 to 9 of Article 670 and Article 670a do not apply if the employee agrees in writing to the termination or if the termination occurs due to the cessation of the activities of the enterprise or of the part of the enterprise in which the employee is exclusively or primarily employed. Article 670, lid 1, introductory phrase and under a, does not apply if the employee who is unable to perform the agreed work due to illness, without valid reason refuses: a. to comply with reasonable instructions given by the employer or an expert designated by him and to cooperate with measures taken by the employer or an expert designated by him to enable him to perform his own or other suitable work; b. to perform suitable work as referred to in Article 658a lid 4 which the employer enables him to perform; c. to cooperate in drawing up, evaluating, and adjusting an action plan as referred to in Article 25, lid 2, of the Work and Income according to Work Capacity Act or Article 71a, lid 2, of the Incapacity for Work Insurance Act."

    [
      "Notice periods (Article 7:672 BW): Termination takes place at the end of the month, unless a different day has been designated by written agreement or custom. The notice period to be observed by the employer for an employment contract that on the day of termination: a. has lasted less than five years: one month; b. has lasted five years or longer, but less than ten years: two months; c. has lasted ten years or longer, but less than fifteen years: three months; d. has lasted fifteen years or longer: four months. The notice period to be observed by the employee is one month. If the consent referred to in Article 6 of the Extraordinary Employment Relations Decree 1945 has been granted, the notice period to be observed by the employer is shortened by one month, provided that the remaining notice period is at least one month. The period, referred to in lid 2, can only be shortened by collective labor agreement or by regulation by or on behalf of a competent administrative body. The period can be extended in writing. The period, referred to in lid 3, can be deviated from in writing. The notice period for the employee may not be longer than six months when extended, and for the employer not shorter than double that for the employee. Lid 4 may be deviated from, insofar as it concerns the remaining notice period of one month, only by collective labor agreement or by regulation by or on behalf of a competent administrative body to the detriment of the employee. By collective labor agreement or by regulation by or on behalf of a competent administrative body, the notice period, referred to in lid 6, second sentence, for the employer may be shortened, provided that the period is not shorter than that for the employee. For the application of lid 2, employment contracts are deemed to form a single, uninterrupted employment contract in case of reinstatement of the employment contract under Article 682."
