    [
      "End of employment contract (Article 7:677 BW): Each party is entitled to terminate the employment contract immediately for an urgent reason, with simultaneous notification of that reason to the other party. The party who terminates without an urgent reason or without simultaneous notification of the urgent reason is liable for damages. The party who terminates against an earlier day than applies between the parties is liable for damages. Also liable for damages is the party who, by intent or fault, has given the other party an urgent reason to terminate the employment contract immediately, if the other party has made use of that right or the court has dissolved the employment contract on that ground under Article 685. In case one of the parties is liable for damages, the other party has the choice to claim the full damages mentioned in Article 680 or a full damages. Failure to observe Article 670 lids 1 to 9, or Article 670a does not make the employer liable for damages. In those cases, the employee can invoke the ground for annulment within two months after the termination of the employment contract. The invocation of the ground for annulment takes place by notification to the employer. Article 55 of Book 3 does not apply."
    ],
    [
      "Urgent reasons for employer (Article 7:678 BW): Such acts, qualities, or behaviors of the employee are considered urgent reasons in the sense of lid 1 of Article 677, which result in the employer reasonably not being able to be required to continue the employment contract. Urgent reasons will, among other things, be deemed to exist: a. when the employee, upon concluding the agreement, misled the employer by presenting false or falsified testimonials, or intentionally provided false information about how his previous employment contract ended; b. when he proves to seriously lack the competence or suitability for the work for which he committed himself; c. when he, despite warning, indulges in drunkenness or other dissolute behavior; d. when he commits theft, embezzlement, fraud, or other crimes, making him unworthy of the employer's trust; e. when he assaults, grossly insults, or seriously threatens the employer, his family members or household members, or his fellow employees; f. when he seduces or attempts to seduce the employer, his family members or household members, or his fellow employees to acts contrary to the laws or good morals; g. when he intentionally, or despite warning recklessly, damages or seriously endangers the employer's property; h. when he intentionally, or despite warning recklessly, seriously endangers himself or others; i. when he discloses particulars concerning the employer's household or business, which he ought to have kept secret; j. when he persistently refuses to comply with reasonable orders or instructions given to him by or on behalf of the employer; k. when he otherwise grossly neglects the duties imposed on him by the employment contract; l. when he, by intent or recklessness, becomes or remains unable to perform the agreed work. Clauses leaving the decision to the employer as to whether an urgent reason in the sense of Article 677 lid 1 exists are void."
    ],
    [
      "Urgent reasons for employee (Article 7:679 BW): Such circumstances are considered urgent reasons for the employee in the sense of Article 677 lid 1, which result in the employee reasonably not being able to be required to continue the employment contract. Urgent reasons will, among other things, be deemed to exist: a. when the employer assaults, grossly insults, or seriously threatens the employee, his family members or household members, or tolerates such acts being committed by one of his household members or subordinates; b. when he seduces or attempts to seduce the employee, his family members or household members to acts contrary to the laws or good morals, or tolerates such a seduction or attempt to seduce by one of his household members or subordinates; c. when he does not pay the wage at the appointed time; d. when he, where board and lodging have been agreed, does not provide for it properly; e. when he does not provide sufficient work to the employee whose wage has been determined dependent on the results of the work to be performed; f. when he does not provide the agreed assistance, or not to a proper extent, to the employee whose wage has been determined dependent on the results of the work to be performed; g. when he otherwise grossly neglects the duties imposed on him by the employment contract; h. when he, without the nature of the employment contract entailing this, orders the employee, despite his refusal, to perform work in the business of another employer; i. when the continuation of the employment contract for the employee would be associated with serious dangers to life, health, morality, or good name, which were not clear at the time of concluding the employment contract; j. when the employee, due to illness or other causes not attributable to him, becomes unable to perform the agreed work. Clauses leaving the decision to the employee as to whether an urgent reason in the sense of Article 677 lid 1 exists are void."
    ],
    [
      "Dissolution of employment contract (Article 7:685 BW): Each party is at all times entitled to apply to the subdistrict court for dissolution of the employment contract for serious reasons. Any clause excluding or limiting this right is void. The subdistrict court can only grant the request if it has ascertained whether the request is related to the existence of a prohibition on termination as referred to in Articles 647, 648, 670, and 670a or any other prohibition on termination of the employment contract. Circumstances that would have constituted an urgent reason as referred to in Article 677 lid 1 if the employment contract had been terminated immediately for that reason, as well as changes in circumstances of such a nature that the employment contract should reasonably end immediately or after a short period, are considered serious reasons. The request is made to the competent subdistrict court under Articles 99, 100, and 107 to 109 of the Code of Civil Procedure. The request must state the place where the work is usually performed, as well as the name and place of residence or, in the absence of a place of residence in the Netherlands, the actual residence of the other party. The subdistrict court may, if the request is linked to a case already pending between the same persons before another court, refer the case to that other court. The clerk sends a copy of the decision, as well as the request and the other documents of the case, for further processing to the court to which it has been referred. The proceedings commence no later than in the fourth week following that in which the request was filed. If the court grants the request, it determines the time at which the employment contract ends. If the court grants the request due to changes in circumstances, it may, if it deems it equitable in view of the circumstances of the case, award compensation to one of the parties at the expense of the other party; it may allow the compensation to be paid in installments in a manner to be determined by it. Before pronouncing a dissolution to which compensation is attached, the court informs the parties of its intention and sets a period within which the applicant has the right to withdraw his request. If the applicant does so, the court will only rule on the costs of the proceedings. Lid 9 applies mutatis mutandis if the court intends to pronounce a dissolution without attaching compensation requested by the applicant. No appeal or cassation can be lodged against a decision under this article."

      "Work and rest times (Article 6.7): Insofar as this CAO does not provide otherwise, the provisions of the Working Hours Act (Atw) and the Working Hours Decree (Atb) apply. The employer communicates the work and rest time pattern to the employee at least 28 days in advance. If, due to the nature of the work, the work and rest time pattern cannot be communicated to the employee at least 28 days in advance, the employer communicates to the employee at least 28 days in advance on which day the weekly rest period begins and on which Sundays the employee does not have to work. In addition, the employer informs the employee at least four days in advance of the times when they must perform work. The work and rest times are determined by the employer, with the understanding that working hours preferably fall between 07:00 and 20:00 from Monday to Friday, and between 8:00 and 12:00 on Saturday. If there is a work and rest time arrangement with alternating shifts, these shifts should rotate forward as much as possible. Shifts that include hours between 23:00 and 07:00, wholly or partially, can only be assigned to employees aged 18 and older. Shifts are performed consecutively, unless the nature of the work associated with the function opposes this. If, in the employer's opinion, such circumstances exist, they will present this to the employee for discussion with reasons before establishing non-consecutive shifts. Handover of duties takes place within working hours."
    ],
    [
      "Breaks (Article 6.8): In addition to the statutory break according to the Working Hours Act, the employer gives the employee the opportunity for additional breaks, namely 15 minutes per 4 hours worked (morning, afternoon, evening, and/or night). Breaks lasting less than a quarter of an hour are considered working hours. If breaks of fifteen minutes or longer are considered working hours, uninterrupted rest during these breaks must be guaranteed."

      "Maximum working hours (Article 6.10): The working hours per shift, excluding overtime, are a maximum of ten hours, including overtime, twelve hours. The working hours per night shift, excluding overtime, are a maximum of nine hours, including overtime, ten hours. The working hours per week are a maximum of sixty hours. The employer does not use the possibilities offered by the Atw (Article 5:8 lid 3) and the Atb (Articles 4.7:1, 4.7:2, and 5.20:2) to extend the working hours per shift, per night shift, or per week. The working hours per four weeks are a maximum of an average of 55 hours per week, and per sixteen weeks, a maximum of an average of 48 hours per week. The working hours per sixteen weeks are a maximum of an average of forty hours per week if the employee performs sixteen or more night shifts in a period of sixteen weeks. For doctors in this situation, the working hours are a maximum of an average of 48 hours per week in a period of thirteen weeks."

If the employer makes a change to an already established work and rest time pattern and a shift occurs within 24 hours after the employee has been informed of this, the employee receives, in addition to the hourly wage for the hours of that shifted shift, only an overtime surcharge percentage as referred to in Article 9.4 lid 2. The exceeding is measured on a half-yearly basis, calculated from the moment of exceeding. Compensation for overtime is given if the employee has received an order to work overtime, or could reasonably assume that they would have received an order to work overtime. In the latter case, the employer retrospectively determines the necessity of overtime."

      "Scope and exempted employees (Article 9.2): If overtime is performed for a period of half an hour or less preceding or immediately following the working hours established in the work and rest time pattern, this period is not eligible for compensation. If overtime is performed for a period longer than half an hour, this period is rounded up to a full hour. If overtime is performed for a period longer than one hour, this period is rounded up to half or full hours. Pregnant employees are not assigned overtime after the third month of pregnancy, unless the employee agrees to perform overtime (Article 4:5 Atw)."

      "Maximum number of overtime hours (Article 9.3): The number of overtime hours may not exceed an average of 10% per week, measured quarterly: a. of 36 hours if the employee's salary does not exceed IP number 48; b. of 42 hours if the employee's salary exceeds IP number 48, but does not exceed IP number 74 of that table; c. of 52 hours if the employee's salary exceeds IP number 74. For employees with working hours deviating from the full-time norm, the pro-rata principle applies. If the percentage of 10% is exceeded, at the request of the employee concerned, either assistance will be provided or a vacancy will be created. If a part-time worker works more than 10% of their contractual working hours in any quarter, at the request of the employee concerned, a contract for the additional hours will be offered. If the person concerned does not request this, assistance will be provided or a vacancy will be created. The works council receives, upon request, an overview of the overtime hours worked per employee per calendar quarter within a department or group to form an opinion on the policy pursued regarding vacancy creation or assistance provision."

      "Compensation scheme for full-time employees (Article 9.4): Compensation for overtime is provided, unless lid 3 provides otherwise, in the form of free time, equal to the number of hours the overtime lasted, and additionally in the form of a monetary payment as referred to in lid 2. The monetary payment consists of a percentage of the hourly wage: 25% for overtime worked between 18:00 and 22:00 from Monday to Friday, with the understanding that in a period of seven days, the number of hours to be compensated in this way is a maximum of five; the remaining hours are compensated at 50%; 50% for overtime worked between 22:00 and 06:00 from Monday to Friday; 35% for overtime worked on Saturday until 24:00 and on free days; 100% for overtime worked on Sunday between 00:00 and 24:00 and on public holidays between 00:00 and 24:00 and on December 24 and 31 between 18:00 and 24:00. For the purpose of this article, free days are understood to mean: days, not being a Sunday or public holiday, on which the employee would not have to work according to their work and rest time pattern. The right to overtime compensation as mentioned in lid 1 is granted if: a. the employee's salary does not exceed IP number 48; b. the employee's salary exceeds IP number 48, but does not exceed IP number 74 of that table: if and insofar as the number of hours worked above the working hours included in the work and rest time pattern amounts to more than six on average per week, measured over the period for which the work and rest time pattern applies; c. the employee's salary is above IP number 74: if and insofar as the number of hours worked above the working hours included in the work and rest time pattern amounts to more than sixteen on average per week, measured over the period for which the work and rest time pattern applies."

      "Definitions of on-call, availability, and standby duty (Article 11.1): On-call duty (bereikbaarheidsdienst) is understood to mean a continuous period of a maximum of 24 hours during which the employee, in addition to performing the agreed work, is obliged to be reachable to perform the agreed work as soon as possible upon call. Availability duty (aanwezigheidsdienst) is understood to mean a continuous period of a maximum of 24 hours during which the employee, in addition to performing the agreed work, is obliged to be present at the workplace to perform the agreed work as soon as possible upon call. Standby duty (consignatie) is understood to mean a period between two consecutive shifts or during a break, during which the employee is exclusively obliged to be reachable to perform the agreed work as soon as possible upon call in case of unforeseen circumstances. For doctors in training (aios), Article 6.13 stipulates that hours worked during on-call, availability, and standby duties count towards determining the normative work week."

      "Working hours during on-call, availability, and standby duty (Article 11.3): If the employee is called out during availability duty, a period of at least half an hour is used for the calculation of compensation; if this call-out occurs during on-call duty or standby duty, a period of at least half an hour is used for the said calculation, to be increased by the actual travel time. If the percentage of 10% is exceeded, at the request of the employee concerned, either assistance will be provided or a vacancy will be created. If a part-time worker works more than 10% of their contractual working hours in any quarter, at the request of the employee concerned, a contract for the additional hours will be offered. If the person concerned does not request this, assistance will be provided or a vacancy will be created. The works council receives, upon request, an overview of the hours worked per employee per calendar quarter under Article 11.1 within a department or group to form an opinion on the policy pursued regarding vacancy creation or assistance provision."
